tafasitamab-cxix
Monjuvi
CD-19 directed cytolytic antibody
MOR208
MorphoSys US Inc.
Injection
The recommended dose of tafasitamab is 12 mg/kg as an
intravenous infusion given according to the following schedule:
x Cycle 1: Days 1, 4, 8, 15 and 22 of the 28-day cycle
x Cycle 2 and 3: Days 1, 8, 15 and 22 of each 28-day cycle
x Cycle 4 and beyond: Days 1 and 15 of each 28-day cycle
(b) (4)

Applicant Proposed
In combination with lenalidomide
(b) (4)
Indication(s)/Population(s)
for the treatment of adult patients with relapsed
or refractory DLBCL, including DLBCL arising from low grade
(b) (4)
lymphoma, and who are not eligible for
ASCT
Recommendation on
Accelerated approval
Regulatory Action
Recommended
In combination with lenalidomide for the treatment of adult
Indication(s)/Population(s) patients with relapsed or refractory diffuse large B-cell
(if applicable)
lymphoma (DLBCL) not otherwise specified, including DLBCL
arising from low grade lymphoma, and who are not eligible for
autologous stem cell transplant (ASCT)

BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation BLA 761163
Monjuvi (tafasitamab-cxix)

1 Executive Summary
Product Introduction
Tafasitamab is an Fc-modified antibody that binds to CD19 antigen expressed on the surface of
pre-B and mature B lymphocytes and on several B-cell malignancies, including diffuse large Bcell lymphoma. Upon binding to CD19, tafasitamab mediates B-cell lysis through apoptosis and
immune effector mechanisms, including ADCC and ADCP.
The recommended dose of tafasitamab is 12 mg/kg as an intravenous infusion given according
to the following schedule:
x Cycle 1: Days 1, 4, 8, 15 and 22 of the 28-day cycle
x Cycle 2 and 3: Days 1, 8, 15 and 22 of each 28-day cycle
x Cycle 4 and beyond: Days 1 and 15 of each 28-day cycle
Tafasitamab is administered in combination with lenalidomide for a maximum of 12 cycles and
then tafasitamab is continued as monotherapy until disease progression or unacceptable
toxicity.

Conclusions on the Substantial Evidence of Effectiveness
The review team recommends accelerated approval of tafasitamab in combination with
lenalidomide for:
“The treatment of adult patients with relapsed or refractory diffuse large B-cell lymphoma
(DLBCL) not otherwise specified, including DLBCL arising from low grade lymphoma, and who
are not eligible for autologous stem cell transplant (ASCT).“
This recommendation is based on a favorable benefit risk profile from trial, MOR208C203, a
multicenter single arm trial of tafasitamab at the proposed dose and schedule in combination
with lenalidomide 25 mg administered for up to 12 cycles in patients with relapsed or refractory
DLBCL.
Trial MOR208C203 (L-MIND) enrolled 81 patients with relapsed or refractory DLBCL based on
local pathology. Patients had relapsed or refractory DLBCL after 1 to 3 prior systemic therapies,
including a CD20-directed cytolytic antibody, and were not candidates for high dose
chemotherapy (HDC) followed by ASCT. The primary efficacy population was a total of 71
patients who had confirmed DLBCL based on central pathology. The median age was 71 years

and on several B-cell malignancies, including diffuse large B-cell lymphoma. Upon binding to CD19, tafasitamab mediates B-cell
lysis through apoptosis and immune effector mechanisms, including ADCC and ADCP.
Efficacy: Efficacy of tafasitamab in patients with relapsed or refractory diffuse large B cell lymphoma (DLBCL) is based on results
from a single, multicenter, single arm trial, MOR208C203 (L-MIND) in 71 patients with relapsed or refractory DLBCL confirmed by
central pathology review. dŚĞ ŵĞĚŝĂŶ ŶƵŵďĞƌ ŽĨ ƉƌŝŽƌ ƚŚĞƌĂƉŝĞƐ ǁĂƐ ƚǁŽ͖ ϰϵй ŚĂĚ ŽŶĞ ƉƌŝŽƌ ůŝŶĞ ŽĨ ƚƌĞĂƚŵĞŶƚ͕ ĂŶĚ ϱϭй ŚĂĚ Ϯ ƚŽ

Benefit-Risk: The available data provides evidence of the benefit risk of tafasitamab in combination with lenalidomide for the
treatment of adult patients with relapsed or refractory DLBCL not otherwise specified, including DLBCL arising from low grade
lymphoma, and who are not eligible for ASCT. The intended population has very few, if any, treatment options, and toxicities with
tafasitamab and lenalidomide combination tended to be manageable with supportive care and dose modifications. The risks and
the benefit/risk profile are acceptable for the intended population. The final patient population will reflect the types of DLBCL
lymphoma enrolled, including DLBCL not otherwise specified and DLBCL arising from low grade lymphoma.
The limitations of the data are 1) the uncertainty of the magnitude of effect due to the small sample size and lack of a comparator
arm 2) the limitations of the retrospective observational data referenced, 3) and the data collected on time-to-event endpoints
are not interpretable, therefore, it cannot be determined if PFS or OS are prolonged. A post-marketing requirement to confirm
(b) (4)
the benefit of tafasitamab in patients with
DLBCL in a randomized controlled trial will be issued.

Gascoyne, 2015). Patients with disease that has relapsed or is refractory to R-CHOP have a poor
prognosis with short survival times, ranging 6-12 months (Coiffier and Sarkozy, 2016; Smith et
al., 2015, Crump et al 2017, Farooq et al., 2017). For the minority of patients who are fit for
high-intensity therapy, it may be possible to achieve cure using intensive re-induction
immunochemotherapy, followed by high-dose chemotherapy (HDC) with subsequent
autologous stem cell transplantation (ASCT). In transplant-eligible R/R DLBCL patients, cure is
ĂĐŚŝĞǀĞĚ ŝŶ ĂƉƉƌŽǆŝŵĂƚĞůǇ ϭϬй ŽĨ ĐĂƐĞƐ͘ dŚĞ ƌĞŵĂŝŶŝŶŐ ϵϬй ǁŝůů ĞǀĞŶƚƵĂůůǇ ƌĞůĂƉƐĞ ĂŐĂŝŶ ĂŶĚ
succumb to their disease (Van Den Neste et al., 2016).
The majority of patients with relapsed/refractory (R/R) DLBCL are not eligible for intensive
immunochemotherapy and ASCT, generally due to advanced age or co-morbidities. The NCCN
guidelines recommend that these or patients who relapse after ASCT participate in a clinical
trial or receive third-line systemic therapies, palliative involved-site radiotherapy or best
supportive care treatments, indicating a lack of effective treatment options. The development
of novel therapies is required due to the continued unmet medical need in patients with R/R
DLBCL.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA agrees with the Applicant’s assessment above.

Analysis of Current Treatment Options
R-CHOP is the standard of care for all newly-ĚŝĂŐŶŽƐĞĚ ƉĂƚŝĞŶƚƐ͖ ĂƉƉƌŽǆŝŵĂƚĞůǇ ϰϬй ŽĨ patients
will ultimately relapse or progress (Vitolo et al., 2017). In patients whose disease progresses or
relapses after first-line treatment, salvage chemotherapy options such as DHAP (cisplatin,
cytarabine, dexamethasone), DHAX (dexamethasone, cytarabine, oxaliplatin), ICE (ifosfamide,
carboplatin, etoposide), or GDP (gemcitabine, dexamethasone, cisplatin/carboplatin), with or
without the anti-CD20 mAb rituximab followed by HDC with ASCT offer the potential for cure.
HDC/ASCT significantly improves disease-free survival and overall survival (OS) in patients with
relapsed / refractory DLBCL (Gisselbrecht et al., 2010; Philip et al., 1995).
However, HDC/ASCT may not be appropriate, especially in patients who are chemorefractory,
have comorbidities or are older, and this applies to a significant proportion of patients with R/R
DLBCL. ƉƉƌŽǆŝŵĂƚĞůǇ Ϯϱй of patients with R/R DLBCL undergo HDC/ASCT . For the remaining
patient population, several second-line combination therapies such as B-R, R-GemOx,
R-Lenalidomide and other therapies as per NCCN guidelines are used with variable success.
Recently, three new therapies have been approved for DLBCL patients who have progressed
after second-line therapy. These comprise two chimeric antigen receptor (CAR) T-cell therapies
(CAR-T therapies), axicabtagene ciloleucel (Yescarta™) and tisagenlecleucel (Kymriah™) as well
as the antibody-drug conjugate polatuzumab vedotin, in combination with bendamustine and
rituximab (Pola + BR). Both CAR-T cell therapies target CD19, the same antigen as tafasitamab,
whereas polatuzumab vedotin targets the CD79b antigen.

The Applicant’s Position:
The CAR-T cell therapies may produce durable CRs, but their use has limitations because of the
toxicity profile, which requires rigorous selection criteria and inpatient administration, and
restricted availability in tertiary care facilities. Additionally, the waiting period associated with
CAR-T manufacture may be prohibitive in patients with symptomatic or rapidly progressing
disease. Polatuzumab vedotin received accelerated approval for use in combination with
bendamustine and rituximab (pola-BR). All three recently approved products are licensed for
use in the third-line setting (after failure of two or more prior systemic therapies). There is no
FDA-approved therapy for patients with R/R DLBCL after failure of one prior therapy, or for
patients who are unable to receive CAR-T therapy.
The FDA’s Assessment:
There is no FDA approved therapy for patients with R/R DLBCL after one prior therapy or
ineligible for autologous stem cell transplant. FDA also agrees that although these CAR-T cell
therapies can produce durable CRs, their use has limitations in the general R/R DLBCL
population. FDA also notes that other combination salvage regimens include platinum,
etoposide, and/or cytarabine based combination regimens and gemcitabine-based regimens.

The FDA’s Assessment:
Relevant discussions from prior meetings are summarized below:
Type C Meeting December 2, 2014
The focus if this meeting was a randomized trial comparing tafasitamab + bendamustine versus
rituximab + bendamustine. The population proposed for this trial was patients who are not
eligible for intensive therapy including SCT. It was discussed that patients who are eligible for
standard therapy but are unwilling or refuse to undergo SCT should not be included in a
registrational trial. The Agency had concerns with the dosing regimen and recommended
before beginning a planned pivotal trial that integrated dose-response and exposure response
analyses should occur.
Type A Meeting August 10, 2015
This meeting was to discuss non-agreement on a Special Protocol Assessment for a protocol of
titled “A Phase III, Randomized, Multicenter Study of MOR00208 with Bendamustine versus
Rituximab with Bendamustine in Patients with Relapsed or Refractory Diffuse Large B-Cell
Lymphoma (R-R DLBCL) Who Are Not Eligible for High-Dose Chemotherapy (HDC) and
Autologous Stem-Cell Transplantation (ASCT). While this is not the trial submitted in support of
this Application, some of the discuss that occurred is relevant and summarized below:
x At this time, the Agency did not agree with a dose of 12 mg/kg as an optimal dose for single
agent use as an integrated dose-response analysis for safety and activity to should be done. It
was discussed the Applicant should investigate the trend of exposure-weight relationship in
order to determine whether weight based dosing or flat dosing was the optimal dosing
regimen.
x Regarding the proposed criterion for ineligibility for SCT it was discussed that age >60 is not a
contraindication to SCT. It was also discussed that some criteria assessed by the treating
physician are nondescript and would be problematic for labeling.

Type C Meeting November 9, 2018
This meeting was focused on the use of Real World Data included with this submission. During
this meeting the following concerns were discussed:
x

x

x

x
x

x

x
x

The data quality from the L-MIND study may differ from the lenalidomide monotherapy
cohort. There may be differences in type of data, missing data and different follow up.
The Applicant was instructed to provide summary data such as the amount of missing
data and duration of follow up to demonstrate comparability. For each data element the
Applicant should provide evidence that the data can be captured adequately, accurately
and non-differentially.
The lenalidomide monotherapy cohort should resemble patients in the L-MIND study. It
was recommended a matching approach be used rather than the overlap weights
approach. The lenalidomide monotherapy cohort should be induced by matching
patients from the database to patients in the L-MIND study. The Agency acknowledged
that this could lead to sample size attrition. However, the Applicant was informed that
substantial attrition is indicative of two very different populations, such that, it may not
be reasonable to compare them.
The concern of covariates, measured or unmeasured, may affect the outcome was also
discussed. The Applicant was informed that when conducting observational studies,
matching on as many reasonable covariates should occur. The Applicant had listed age,
Ann-Arbor stage, IPI score, refractoriness to previous line of therapy, number of prior
lines of therapy, history of primary refractoriness, and prior ASCT. It was mentioned
other covariates such as laboratory values prior to index date should be included.
The Applicant was informed at this time the Agency does not accept imputation of
missing covariates for the purpose of estimating propensity scores.
Concern regarding the collection of response data for assessment of the ORR endpoint
was raised. Data from the clinical notes alone may not allow assessment of different
aspects of response criteria. It was recommended the Applicant consider a validation
study in patients who have clinical notes and imaging to ensure robustness of the data.
Outcome misclassification was also discussed. Is a reported response an actual
response? Is a reported lack of response an actual lack of response? The response rate
in the lenalidomide monotherapy cohort may not be identifiable as it can be
contaminated by the misclassification process. The Applicant was also informed that
misclassification can affect covariates such as medical history, disease, staging, prior
therapies at the time of the lenalidomide monotherapy index date. It was critical that
values at the time of the index date were accurately captured.
For an accurate assessment of the endpoints a minimum duration of follow up of 6
months for each patient included in the monotherapy cohort was recommended.
Only patients with in initial dose of 25 mg of lenalidomide at the index date in the
lenalidomide monotherapy cohort should be included.

5 Nonclinical Pharmacology/Toxicology
Executive Summary
Tafasitamab (also known as MOR00208, MOR208, and XmAb5574) is a humanized IgG1/2
hybrid Fc-modified monoclonal antibody that targets CD19, a cell surface protein expressed on
B cells and follicular dendritic cells of the hematopoietic system and on several B-cell
malignancies including DLBCL 1, 2.
Tafasitamab bound to human CD19 antigen with an average dissociation constant (KD) of
approximately 0.4 nM. Comparable binding of tafasitamab was observed to CD19-expressing
lymphocytes of human, cynomolgus monkey, and rhesus monkey, and low-affinity binding to
dog, rabbit, rat, and mouse lymphocytes. Tafasitamab exhibited similar binding affinities to
human and monkey Fc gamma receptor (FcyRs). There was no off-target binding of tafasitamab
detected in tissue panels from the human and cynomolgus monkey. Tafasitamab bound to
mononuclear leukocytes in the majority of tissues examined, lymphocytes in blood, and
hematopoietic precursor cells in the bone marrow in both species. Based on these findings, the
cynomolgus monkey was chosen as the relevant toxicology species.
The in vitro activity of tafasitamab was assessed in human B-cell lines, as well as primary cells
derived from patients with hematological malignancies. Upon binding to CD19, tafasitamab
mediates B-cell lysis of tumor cells through apoptosis, mainly antibody dependent cellular
cytotoxicity (ADCC), and antibody-dependent cell-mediated phagocytosis (ADCP). Tafasitamab
showed ADCC activity in several CD19+ lymphoma cell lines including DLBCL; with NK-mediated

с ϱϰϳ͘ϰ ʅŐͬŵ>͖ DĞĂŶ h0-last с ϵϭϰ͘ϯ ʅŐͼĚĂǇͬŵ>Ϳ͘
Based on a prior agreement with the FDA, the Applicant did not conduct reproductive and
developmental toxicity studies with tafasitamab since tafasitamab is administered in
combination with lenalidomide, a known teratogen and embryo-fetal toxicant. The Applicant
submitted other information in the weight-of-evidence (WOE) reproductive risk assessment on
the potential effects of tafasitamab (monotherapy) on embryofetal development, consistent
with the Oncology Pharmaceuticals: Reproductive Toxicity Testing and Labeling
Recommendations Guidance for Industry. Based on the WOE risk assessment, treatment of
pregnant women with tafasitamab may result in depletion of B cells and thus dysfunction of
immune response in a newborn. This risk has been communicated in the tafasitamab label.
Normal B-cell counts and immunologic function may be restored based on reversibility of
effects in animal studies. The contraception duration listed in the label is until at least 3 months
after the last dose and covers a period of at least 5 half-lives for tafasitamab (T1/2= 17 days).
Additionally, lactating women are advised not to breastfeed during treatment and for 3 months
after the last dose.
Carcinogenicity studies are not necessary to support the marketing of therapeutics intended to
treat patients with advanced cancer as discussed in the ICH S9 guidance document.

Genotoxicity studies are generally not necessary to support marketing of biotechnology-derived
pharmaceuticals such as tafasitamab as discussed in the ICH S6 guidance document.
The nonclinical studies submitted to this BLA provide sufficient information to support
the use of tafasitamab (Monjuvi), in combination with lenalidomide, for the treatment of adult
patients with relapsed or refractory diffuse large B-cell lymphoma (DLBCL). From the
nonclinical pharmacology and toxicology perspective, tafasitamab (Monjuvi) is recommended
for approval.

Referenced NDAs, BLAs, DMFs
The Applicant’s Position:
There are no referenced NDAs, BLAs, or DMFs related to nonclinical pharmacology or toxicology
for tafasitamab.

Pharmacology
Primary Pharmacology
The FDA agrees with the primary pharmacology data summarized by the Applicant that
describes tafasitamab’ s pharmacological effects including ADCC, ADCP activities, apoptosis of
tumor cells and the increased anti-tumor activities in combination with lenalidomide.
Tafasitamab does not mediate complement-dependent cytotoxicity (CDC).
Binding affinity of tafasitamab to human CD19 was measured in cell-based formats (study
#TR07098). Tafasitamab bound to CD19-expressing human embryonic kidney (HEK) 293 cells
transfected with a human CD19 expression plasmid in a concentration-dependent manner with
an observed half-maximal concentration (EC50) of 0.72 μg/mL, or 4.8 nM. CD19-expressing
human tumor cell lines (Raji and RS4;11), were used to determine the steady-state cell binding
by measuring the equilibrium dissociation constant (KD) by flow cytometry. The steady-state cell
binding assays indicated that tafasitamab binds cell-surface CD19 in the range of 0.29 to 0.46
nM, resulting in an average KD of approximately 0.4 nM.
Binding of tafasitamab to CD19-expressing lymphocytes from multiple mammalian species was
assessed to identify the relevant species for toxicological evaluation (study # XC9-23209).
Whole blood samples from human donors, cynomolgus and rhesus monkeys, dogs, rabbits, rats,
and mice were evaluated in flow cytometric studies using fluorescein isothiocyanate conjugated
(FITC) tafasitamab. Binding of tafasitamab-FITC to human, cynomolgus monkey and rhesus
monkey lymphocytes was comparable. Tafasitamab showed lower binding to rat and mouse,
and no binding to dog and rabbit lymphocytes.

The in vitro ADCC of tafasitamab in combination with lenalidomide (LEN) against DLBCL cell
lines was investigated (MOR208L039). The immunomodulatory drug lenalidomide activates T
cells to release the cytokines interferon gamma (IFN-ɶͿ and interleukin-2 (IL-2), which stimulate
NK cell activity and lead to increased NK cell numbers 4,5 . Consequently, treatment in
combination with lenalidomide appears to increase tafasitamab-mediated NK-cell activation and
ADCC activity 6.
The antitumor activity of tafasitamab, LEN or the combination of both was determined with
peripheral blood mononuclear cells (PBMCs) isolated from healthy donors as effector cells and
DLBCL cell lines (SU-DHL-2, -4, -6 and U-2932) as target cells. PBMCs were preincubated for 72
hours in the presence of 10 μM LEN or DMSO (vehicle control). Subsequently, the preincubated cells were utilized in a FACS-based cytotoxicity assay in the presence or absence of
tafasitamab to compare the cytotoxic effects of single agent or combination. Cytotoxicity was
measured by quantification of dead and viable cells using the fluorescent DNA binding dye 4',6diamidino-2-phenylindole (DAPI).

The lifespan of animals after treatment with tafasitamab in combination with lenalidomide was

mg/kg as described above. Vehicle control: Ora-Plus:Ora-Sweet SF (1:1) was administered orally
for 28 days, and the isotype control antibody Ref_mAb_33 was administered i.v. twice weekly
for three weeks. Following the completion of treatment, the animals were monitored for
survival for five weeks post-inoculation. The lifespan was increased in the combination groups
compared to the respective mono-therapy groups.

The Applicant’s Position:
Tafasitamab is an Fc-enhanced monoclonal antibody that targets the CD19 antigen. Two amino
acid modifications were introduced into the Fc-domain of tafasitamab leading to increased
ĂĨĨŝŶŝƚǇ ƚŽ &Đɶ ƌĞĐĞƉƚŽƌƐ͕ resulting in more potent immune effector cell mechanisms including
enhanced ADCC and ADCP, compared with an antibody with an unmodified Fc-domain.
In vitro assays demonstrated that upon binding to CD19, tafasitamab mediates B-cell lysis
ƚŚƌŽƵŐŚ͗ ϭͿ ĞŶŐĂŐĞŵĞŶƚ ŽĨ ŝŵŵƵŶĞ ĞĨĨĞĐƚŽƌ ĐĞůůƐ͕ ůŝŬĞ E< ĐĞůůƐ͕ ɶɷ d ĐĞůůƐ ĂŶĚ ƉŚĂŐŽĐǇƚĞƐ͖ ĂŶĚ,
2) direct induction of cell death (apoptosis). Tafasitamab does not mediate CDC.
The in vivo antitumor activity of tafasitamab monotherapy was evaluated using subcutaneous
xenograft models in severe combined immunodeficiency (SCID) mice engrafted with human Raji
and RAMOS lymphoma cells. Tafasitamab treatment resulted in reduction in tumor growth.
Lenalidomide exerts its antitumor activity by inhibition of proliferation and apoptosis as well via
its immunostimulatory function to activate T- and NK cells (REVLIMID USPI). As NK cells are an
important effector cell population for tafasitamab mediated ADCC, the combination of
tafasitamab and lenalidomide was investigated preclinically. Increased anti-tumor activity in in
vitro ADCC assays and increased survival in a xenograft in vivo model of disseminated RAMOS
cells, compared with both monotherapy regimens, were observed. These data provided the
rationale to investigate the combination of tafasitamab and lenalidomide in MOR208C203
(L-MIND).

Reference ID: 4650005

BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation BLA 761163
Monjuvi (tafasitamab-cxix)
Secondary Pharmacology
No secondary pharmacodynamic effect unrelated to CD19 binding were observed, thus no
dedicated secondary pharmacodynamics studies have been conducted.
Tafasitamab cross-reacts with CD19 on cynomolgus monkey B-lymphocytes. In cynomolgus
monkeys, tafasitamab mediated B-cell depletion from peripheral blood as a secondary
pharmacodynamic effect at doses ranging from 0.3 mg/kg to 100 mg/kg was observed.
Histopathological changes to several lymphatic organs (decreased cellularity of the germinal
centers in lymphoid follicles of spleen, mesenteric and mandibular lymph nodes) were
observed. B-cell depletion in peripheral blood was reversible upon discontinuation of
tafasitamab in cynomolgus monkeys.
The Applicant’s Position:
The reduced peripheral B-cell counts and changes to lymphatic organs are considered
exaggerated pharmacodynamic effects related to B-cell depletion.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA agrees with the Applicant that tafasitamab-related changes to lymphatic organs are
exaggerated pharmacodynamic effects related to B-cell depletion. In the GLP tissue-cross
reactivity studies with human and cynomolgus monkey tissues, tafasitamab binds only B-cells
(as expected based on the expression profile of CD19).
Safety Pharmacology
The Applicant’s Position:
No stand-alone safety pharmacology studies were conducted. The safety pharmacology
parameters were assessed within the pivotal 8-week and 13-week repeat-dose toxicity studies
in cynomolgus monkey (XC-9-14408, MOR208P008) and did not reveal any unwanted effects on
vital organ functions (cardiovascular, respiratory, central nervous system) at doses up to
100 mg/kg.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The neurobehavioral assessments in the 13-week repeat-dose toxicology study in monkeys
revealed severe tremors of the whole body observed occasionally in one out five females at the
high dose during the tafasitamab 60-minute infusion as well as slight tremors for several
animals at the control, low-dose and high-dose. The number of animals affected and the
number of incidences of tremors were higher at the high-dose. The tremors resolved following
the termination of dosing. In addition, statistically significant differences in body temperature

(100-102°F) were observed in males at 100 mg/kg and females at 30 and 100 mg/kg during

adverse effects at 100 mg/kg.

BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation BLA 761163
Monjuvi (tafasitamab-cxix)

ADME/PK
The Applicant’s Position:
Following i.v. administration in five separate studies in cynomolgus monkey at dose levels from
0.3 – 100 mg/kg, tafasitamab exhibited a typical 2-compartment PK profile. Tafasitamab
showed consistent PK parameters across studies and study groups (volume of distribution at
steady state (Vss) 52.6 mL/kg - 124.3 mL/kg, Clearance (CL) 4.31 mL/day/kg - 7.37 mL/day/kg,
mean terminal elimination half-life 7.7 days - 14.2 days). Dose linearity was observed between
2-100 mg/kg, and no gender differences were determined. Exposure values (AUC and Cmax) at
NOAEL (100mg/kg) were 8-9 fold higher than corresponding values in humans. As expected for
a humanized monoclonal antibody in non-human primates, the development of anti-drug
antibodies (ADA) was observed, which was associated with increased tafasitamab clearance but
not with any other safety relevant laboratory or clinical findings. An additional exploratory PK
study in mice was performed confirming the 2-compartment PK profile and demonstrating PK
parameters as expected for humanized therapeutic antibodies in mice without TMDD.

Toxicology
General Toxicology
The Applicant’s Position:
The toxicological assessment was performed in cynomolgus monkeys and included pivotal
8-week (XC-9-14408)and 13-week (MOR208P008) repeat-dose IV infusion toxicity studies. In
summary, tafasitamab was well tolerated after weekly IV administration to cynomolgus
monkeys at doses up to 100 mg/kg for 13 weeks, with no target organ toxicities or mortalities.
Administration of tafasitamab was not associated with electrocardiographic evidence of
cardiotoxicity, arrhythmogenesis, or effects on blood pressure when evaluated as part of the
pivotal toxicity studies. Importantly, no evidence of an increased susceptibility to infections was
noted after tafasitamab administration to cynomolgus monkeys for 13 weeks.
The B-cell clearance observed in these pivotal toxicity studies represents the desired
pharmacologic mechanism of action for the proposed therapeutic intervention in B-cell
malignancies.

Study title/ Study number: MOR00208: 13-week intravenous infusion toxicity study in
the cynomolgus monkey with a 132 day recovery period/MOR208P008
Key Study Findings
x Slight to severe tremors were observed during the tafasitamab infusion. In
addition, significant changes were noted in body temperature.
x Tafasitamab caused marked decreases in peripheral blood CD20+ B cells at all
doses.
x Tafasitamab caused reductions in T-cell dependent antibody response (TDAR) as
assessed by immune response to keyhole limpet hemocyanine (KLH) and
Tetanus Toxoid vaccine (TT).
x Lack of germinal centers in lymphoid follicles was observed in several lymphatic
organs (spleen, mesenteric and mandibular lymph node) in all tafasitamabtreated groups during the treatment period.
x There were no effects on menstrual cycle length in females and no
histopathology/microscopic changes to male and female reproductive organs.

Clinical Signs

Major findings
There were no unscheduled deaths during the study.
Whole body (WB) tremors were observed at low-dose, high-dose and
control animals during the dosing period. The number of animals
effected and the number of incidences of tremors were higher at the

on Day 1 of dosing.

Urinalysis

Cytokine Analysis

Immunophenotyping

Unremarkable
Two samples from one animal at 10 mg/kg were confirmed positive for
ADA at Week 1 (predose) and Week 18 of the recovery period. There was
no impact of ADAs on system exposure or clearance in these animals.
It is likely that high levels of the investigational product interfered with
the detection of ADAs.
Female animals of each tafasitamab dose group showed an increase in
interleukin-6 (IL-6) values at 4 hours after the end of the infusion for the
first dose (Day 1) and the third dose (Day 15). The increase was less
pronounced after the third dose.
All doses depleted peripheral B cells (CD3- CD20+) throughout the dosing
period; effects were reversible as assessed at the end of the recovery
period (Week 19). Absolute NK cells decreased during the first week in
several animals, and returned to the range of predose for the rest of the
dosing period. Consistent with B-cell depletion observed in peripheral
blood, reductions in CD20+ B cells in bone marrow, lymph node and
spleen were observed, and were reversible.

Toxicokinetics

See the section 1.4 ADME/PK for the toxicokinetic data.
General toxicology; additional studies
Tafasitamab was also assessed in three non-GLP single-dose studies (study #XC9-05707, #XC907907, #XC0-08007), and a GLP 8-week repeat-dose study (XC9-14408), each in cynomolgus
monkeys. The results are summarized below. In general, findings were comparable to those
observed in the 13-week toxicology study and expected based on the pharmacology of the
product. See additional information below related to the 8-week toxicology, Study XC9-14408:
In a GLP-compliant study, groups of 5/sex cynomolgus monkeys received 2, 10, or 50 mg/kg of
tafasitamab by i.v. infusion once every 2 weeks x 4. Three animals/sex were necropsied at
termination of dosing (Day 57) and 2/sex were sacrificed after 90 days of recovery (Day 147).
Key Study Findings:
x Tafasitamab caused marked decreases in peripheral blood B cells associated with
decreased absolute lymphocytes, decreased white blood cell counts, decreased spleen
weights, and altered spleen histology (decreased cellularity of the splenic germinal
center). By the end of recovery (Day 90), depleted B cells at the low- and mid-dose had
fully recovered but the high-ĚŽƐĞ ŚĂĚ ŽŶůǇ ƉĂƌƚŝĂůůǇ ƌĞĐŽǀĞƌĞĚ ;ƚŽ ϱϯй ŽĨ ďĂƐĞůŝŶĞͿ͘
x The splenic change was observed at all doses, and the severity increased with increasing
dose. Recovery was apparent; one high-dose animal (1/4, a male) continued to exhibit
decreased cellularity of the germinal center on Day 147.

Genetic Toxicology
The Applicant’s Position:
No studies were conducted to evaluate the genotoxicity of tafasitamab. According to ICH
guideline S6(R1), the range and type of genotoxicity studies routinely conducted for
pharmaceuticals are not applicable to biotechnology-derived pharmaceuticals and therefore
are not needed for tafasitamab. In addition, it is not expected that tafasitamab would interact
directly with DNA or other chromosomal material.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA concurs with the Applicant.
Carcinogenicity
The Applicant’s Position:
Carcinogenicity studies in animals were not conducted with tafasitamab. ICH guideline S9
outlines that carcinogenicity studies are not warranted to support marketing for therapeutics
intended to treat patients with advanced cancer, and as such no carcinogenicity studies are
required.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA concurs with the Applicant.

Reproductive and Developmental Toxicology
The Applicant’s Position:
In accordance with ICH S9, no embryofetal development or (enhanced) pre- and postnatal
development studies were conducted, as the drug is given in the intended patient population in
combination with a drug known to be teratogenic or causing embryo-fetal lethality
(lenalidomide). The approach was agreed upon with FDA.
Potential effects of tafasitamab on male and female fertility were examined by standard
histopathology evaluation of the reproductive tract and by menstrual cycle assessment in
females during the pivotal 13-week, repeat-dose, toxicity study in sexually mature cynomolgus
monkeys (MOR208P008); no drug-related changes were observed.

Monjuvi (tafasitamab-cxix)
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA concurs with the Applicant that no embryo-fetal development (EFD) studies are
needed when tafasitamab is given in combination with lenalidomide, a known teratogen and
embryo-fetal toxicant.
To address the potential effects of tafasitamab on embryo-fetal development, the Applicant
submitted an assessment for reproductive and developmental risk using a weight-of-evidence
approach and with the Oncology Pharmaceuticals: Reproductive Toxicity Testing and Labeling
Recommendations Guidance for Industry. The relevant information is summarized below and
supports that the main reproductive risk for tafasitamab is B-cell depletion in neonates.
The WOE risk assessment include the following information; references cited were provided by
the Applicant:
1) As CD19 expression is mainly restricted to cells of the B-cell lineage, and dendritic
cells, the main reproductive risk for tafasitamab is immune related. While the impact
of tafasitamab on fetal development is unknown; data in CD19 knockout mice and
SCID mouse deficient in T and B cells, suggest that CD19 is not essential for
developmental processes during embryofetal development 7,8,9,. Tafasitamab is an
IgG monoclonal antibody, and as a class, IgG monoclonal antibodies have the
potential to cross the placental barrier permitting direct fetal exposure. Based on its
mechanism of action and pharmacodynamic effects seen in animals, tafasitamab
may cause depletion of B cells and impair the immune response after birth due to
fetal exposure to tafasitamab.
2) As described in toxicology studies in monkeys, the main toxicities of tafasitamab
treatment were to the immune system with no evidence of off-target toxicity,
including effects to the male and female reproductive organs.
3) The dosing regimen for the intended patient population (relapsed or refractory
diffuse large B-cell lymphoma) consists of the combination of tafasitamab and
lenalidomide. Lenalidomide is a teratogenic drug.

Monjuvi (tafasitamab-cxix)
Other Toxicology Studies
The Applicant’s Position:
To identify tissues with the potential to be associated with tafasitamab systemic toxicities, the
binding of FITC labelled tafasitamab to cynomolgus monkey and human tissue panels was
evaluated using immune-histochemical staining in frozen sections of normal tissues.
Tafasitamab-FITC bound both to lymphocytes in the blood, and to mononuclear leukocytes
which are presumed to be B-lymphocytes based on morphology and/or location, in numerous
tissues. Some hematopoietic precursor cells in the bone marrow also stained. Under the
conditions of the study, it was not possible to determine the stage of B-cell development for
these precursor cells. No off-target binding was detected in tissue cross-reactivity studies on
normal tissues from human and cynomolgus monkey.
Staining in cynomolgus monkey tissues closely paralleled that seen in human tissues. The cell
types stained, and the subcellular localization of the stain were similar although the staining
intensity and frequency were not clear but may be attributable to the antigenic structure or
distribution differences between species.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA agrees with Applicant.
Compared to the human tissue cross-reactivity study, the binding intensity was reduced for the
monkeys, likely due to a difference in binding affinity to the epitope.

6 Clinical Pharmacology
Executive Summary
The FDA’s Assessment:
Tafasitamab is a Fc-modified monoclonal antibody that binds to CD19 antigen expressed on the
surface of pre-B and mature B lymphocytes, and on several B-cell malignancies, including
diffuse large B-cell lymphoma (DLBCL). The Applicant is seeking approval of tafasitamab in
combination with lenalidomide for the treatment of adult patients with relapsed or refractory
(b)
DLBCL, including DLBCL arising from low grade lymphoma, and who are not eligible for, (4)
(b) (4)
autologous stem cell transplant (ASCT). The proposed dosage of tafasitamab is 12 mg/kg
administered by intravenous infusion according to the following schedule (1 cycle = 28 days):
 Cycle 1: Days 1, 4, 8, 15, and 22
 Cycle 2-3: Days 1, 8, 15, and 22
 Cycle 4 and beyond: Days 1 and 15
Tafasitamab is proposed to be administered in combination with lenalidomide 25 mg on Days 121 of each cycle for a maximum of 12 cycles, followed by tafasitamab monotherapy until
disease progression or unacceptable toxicity.
The Clinical Pharmacology section of the BLA is supported by analyses of single and multiple
dose pharmacokinetics (PK) of tafasitamab in patients with DLBCL, immunogenicity, population
pharmacokinetics (popPK), and exposure-response (E-R) relationships.
RECOMMENDATIONS
The Office of Clinical Pharmacology has reviewed the information and data submitted in BLA
761163. This BLA is approvable from a clinical pharmacology perspective. The key review issues
with specific recommendations/comments are summarized in Table 7 below.

Distribution
The total volume of distribution for tafasitamab was determined at 9.3 L.
Elimination
The general clearance of tafasitamab was determined at 0.41 L/day and terminal elimination
half-life at 16.9 days. Following long-term observations, tafasitamab clearance was found to
decrease over time to a value of 0.19 L/day after two years.

Metabolism
The exact pathway through which tafasitamab is metabolized has not been characterized. As a
humanized IgG monoclonal antibody, tafasitamab is expected to be degraded into small
peptides and amino acids via catabolic pathways in the same manner as endogenous IgG.
Immunogenicity
Overall, no treatment-emergent or treatment-boosted anti-tafasitamab antibodies were

General Dosing and Therapeutic Individualization
6.2.2.1.

General Dosing

The recommended dose of tafasitamab is 12 mg/kg body weight administered as an
intravenous infusion according to the following dosing schedule:
x
x
x
x

Cycle length: 28 days.
Cycle 1: Administer tafasitamab on Day 1, 4, 8, 15 and 22.
Cycles 2 - 3: Administer tafasitamab on Day 1, 8, 15 and 22 of each cycle.
Cycle 4 until disease progression: Administer tafasitamab on Days 1 and 15.

Patients should self-administer lenalidomide capsules at the recommended starting dose of
25 mg daily on Days 1 - 21 of each cycle. Tafasitamab plus lenalidomide in combination are
given up to twelve cycles. Lenalidomide dose adaptations are permitted as per prescribing
information. Refer to the lenalidomide prescribing information for dosing guidelines and
recommendations.

6.2.2.2.

Therapeutic Individualization

In a population PK analysis for tafasitamab (see also below in 0), age (16 to 90 years), body
weight, sex, tumor size, disease type, B-cell or absolute lymphocyte counts, anti-drug
antibodies, lactate dehydrogenase, serum albumin, hepatic (AST, ALT, ALKP, GGT) and renal
function(CrCL) had no clinically significant effect on the PK of tafasitamab. The influence of race
and ethnicity is unknown. Lenalidomide co-administration was not found to influence
tafasitamab PK in the Population PK analysis.
The Applicant’s Position:
No dose adjustments are recommended based on intrinsic or extrinsic factors.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s position that no dose adjustments are recommended based on
intrinsic or extrinsic factors. The popPK analyses showed that body weight (40 to 163 kg) has a
clinically significant effect on the PK of tafasitamab, which supports body weight-based dosing
for tafasitamab. No clinically meaningful differences in the PK of tafasitamab were observed
based on age (16 to 90 years), sex, mild to moderate renal impairment (CLcr 30-89 mL/min,
estimated by the Cockcroft-'ĂƵůƚ ĞƋƵĂƚŝŽŶͿ ĂŶĚ ŵŝůĚ ŚĞƉĂƚŝĐ ŝŵƉĂŝƌŵĞŶƚ ;ƚŽƚĂů ďŝůŝƌƵďŝŶ ч h>E
and AST > ULN, or total bilirubin 1 to 1.5 times ULN and any AST). The effect of severe renal
impairment to end-stage renal disease (CLcr < 30 mL/min), moderate to severe hepatic
impairment (total bilirubin > 1.5 times ULN and any AST), and race/ethnicity on tafasitamab PK
is unknown (although no effect is expected).

6.3. Comprehensive Clinical Pharmacology Review
General Pharmacology and Pharmacokinetic Characteristics
Data:
Table 8: Overview Clinical Pharmacology Characteristics Tafasitamab
General Information
Chemical
Structure

Disulfide-linked glycosylated protein tetramer with two identical 451 amino acid
heavy chains and two identical 219 amino acid kappa light chains
Fc part: human IgG1/2 with two single amino acid exchanges (S239D and I332E)
Molecular weight: approximately 150 kDa

Mechanism of
Action

Tafasitamab binds to the human B-cell surface antigen cluster of differentiation
(CD) 19. The engineered Fc part possesses significantly increased binding to Fc
ŐĂŵŵĂ ƌĞĐĞƉƚŽƌƐ ;&ĐɶZͿ ůĞĂĚŝŶŐ ƚŽ ĞŶŚĂŶĐĞĚ ŝŶ ǀŝƚƌŽ ĂŶƚŝďŽĚǇ-dependent cellular
cytotoxicity (ADCC) and antibody-dependent cellular phagocytosis (ADCP).
Tafasitamab also mediates direct cytotoxic effects (apoptosis) on tumor cells
relative to the unmodified antibody. Tafasitamab does not mediate
complement-dependent cytotoxicity (CDC).

Bioanalysis

64
Version date: July 24, 2019 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)

and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Monjuvi (tafasitamab-cxix)
Bioanalysis PK

Tafasitamab concentrations in human plasma or serum were quantified by
validated ligand binding assays (PK assay). The first-generation PK assay
(enzyme-linked immunosorbent assay - ELISA) employed capture with a mouse
monoclonal anti-tafasitamab idiotypic antibody and detection with an anti-human
IgG Fc antibody. The second-generation PK assay (electrochemiluminescence assay)
employed capture with a human monoclonal anti-idiotypic antibody (Fab fragment)
and detection with a mouse monoclonal anti-tafasitamab idiotypic antibody. The
lower limit of quantification (LLOQ) in undiluted samples was 80 ng/mL and
50 ng/mL in the 1st and 2nd generation PK assay, respectively.

Bioanalysis ADA

Anti-tafasitamab antibodies were determined using a validated homogenous
bridging electrochemiluminescence based ligand-binding assay. For the pivotal
study L-MIND a Solid-Phase Extraction Followed by Acid Dissociation (SPEAD)
approach was added for sample pre-treatment to increase drug tolerance. Study
samples were analyzed using a multi-tiered testing approach consisting of a
screening, a conformation, a specificity and a titer determination assay. For the
pivotal study L-MIND confirmed positive samples were further tested in a validated
cell based ADCC neutralizing antibody (NAb) assay.

Pharmacokinetic (PK) Characteristics
Pop-PK Model

For the population PK analysis incl. covariate assessments results from four
different clinical studies were used (XmAb5574-01, MOR208C201, MOR208C202
and MOR208C203). The PK of tafasitamab following IV administration was well
characterized by a 2-compartment linear disposition model. A time-dependent
clearance term (CL exp( tɶ/(T50ɶ + tɶ))) was included in the model to describe the
increasing trough concentrations observed in the L-MIND study (MOR208C203)
beyond one year of treatment.

Drug Exposure

Based on the population pharmacokinetic analysis, tafasitamab average serum
trough concentrations (± standard deviation) in the L-Mind study were determined

Dose Linearity

Dose linearity was assessed in the Phase I dose escalation study XmAb5574-01. For
dose levels below 3 mg/kg nonlinear pharmacokinetics were observed in patients
with R/R CLL/SLL. Employing dose levels of 3 – 12 mg/kg, AUC and Cmax increased
in a dose proportional manner.

Absorption

No bioavailability studies were performed as tafasitamab is administered IV.

Distribution

The total volume of distribution for tafasitamab was determined at 9.3 L.

65
Version date: July 24, 2019 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the Applicant
and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 4650005

BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation BLA 761163
Monjuvi (tafasitamab-cxix)
Elimination

The general clearance of tafasitamab was determined at 0.41 L/day; terminal
elimination half-life at 16.9 days. Following long-term observations, tafasitamab
clearance was found to decrease over time to a value of 0.19 L/day after two years.

Metabolism

The metabolic pathway of tafasitamab has not been characterized. As a human
monoclonal antibody, tafasitamab is expected to be degraded into small peptides
and amino acids via catabolic pathways in the same manner as endogenous IgGs.

Excretion

The excretion of tafasitamab has not been studied. Tafasitamab is a human
monoclonal IgG1/2 antibody and has a molecular weight of approximately 150 kDa;
therefore, intact tafasitamab is unlikely to be filtered by the kidney or excreted in
the urine.

Pharmacodynamic (PD) characteristics
B-cell depletion Peripheral B-cell depletion was demonstrated in whole blood samples within
clinical studies MOR208C203 (L-MIND), MOR208C201, MOR208C202,
MOR208C205 and XmAb5574-01. In most studies, substantial B-cell depletion was
observed at the earliest consistently tested time point, Cycle 1 Day 8 (C1D8). The
magnitude of the maximal reduction seen at nadir relative to baseline count
ranged from -ϴϬй ;DKZϮϬϴϮϬϭͿ ƚŽ -ϭϬϬй ;DKZϮϬϴϮϬϯ͕ ƚŚĞ ƉŝǀŽƚĂů ƐƚƵĚǇͿ͕

treatment start.
QT effects

As tafasitamab is a human monoclonal antibody no “Thorough QT/QTc Study” was
performed. Instead, periodic ECG monitoring was implemented in all companysponsored clinical studies to capture potential ECG changes. Results showed that
tafasitamab did not have a clinically relevant effect on cardiac repolarization or
result in clinically significant cardiac AEs.

Clinical Pharmacology Questions
6.3.2.1. Does the clinical pharmacology program provide supportive
evidence of effectiveness?
In clinical studies XmAb5574-01, MOR208C201, MOR208C202, L-MIND, and MOR208C205,
tafasitamab treatment led to a reduction in peripheral B-cells in subjects suffering from CLL,
MCL, MZL, ALL and DLBCL with a maximum reduction that generally exceeded -ϵϱй ǀƐ͘
baseline. Substantial B-cell depletion was usually observed after 8 days, reaching a median
nadir within 6 to 16 weeks after treatment start. The depletion was lasting under continued
tafasitamab treatment.
For the dose-exposure-response (DER) analysis for efficacy and safety of tafasitamab, refer to
section 6.3.2.2.
The Applicant’s Position:
Depletion of peripheral B-cells is consistent with the proposed mode of action of tafasitamab as
a mediator of ADCC, ADCP and direct cytotoxicity towards CD19 expressing cells. The
dose-exposure response analysis for efficacy provides evidence for effectiveness.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s position on the time course of pharmacodynamics response.
The primary evidence of effectiveness comes from pivotal Study MOR208C203 (L-MIND) in the

6.3.2.2. Is the proposed dosing regimen appropriate for the general

The dosing regimen for the combination therapy uses the maximum previously applied

Tafasitamab

Version date: July 24, 2019 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)

BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation BLA 761163
Monjuvi (tafasitamab-cxix)
A dose-exposure-response (DER) analysis for efficacy and safety of tafasitamab was performed,
with tafasitamab exposure metrics derived by simulation for each subject from the Pop-PK
model. The individual exposure metrics were separated into exposure quartiles and analyzed
against PFS and ORR, or against treatment-emergent adverse events (TEAEs) and each AE of
special interest (AESI). Furthermore, logistic regression analysis was performed in order assess
the impact of tafasitamab exposure on safety and efficacy.
Exposure-Response Relationship for Clinical Efficacy
A post-hoc assessment of the DER relationship for efficacy (study MOR208L035) was performed
for clinical trials MOR208C203/L-MIND, MOR208C201, and XmAb5574-01 with the following
conclusions:
x

x

x

An exposure-response relationship was observed for tafasitamab monotherapy in clinical
studies XmAb5574-01 (subjects with R/R CLL/SLL) and MOR208C201 (subjects with R/R
NHL/DLBCL, see Figure 7). Within these studies, higher exposure to tafasitamab was
associated with higher ORRs and prolonged median PFS.
In combination with lenalidomide (MOR208C203), tafasitamab exhibited a flat
exposure-response relationship (see Figure 7). Based on the results of the exposureresponse analysis, increasing exposure to tafasitamab is unlikely to result in higher response
rates or longer PFS when tafasitamab is used in combination with lenalidomide.
Switching the dosing frequency of tafasitamab in combination with LEN from initially Q1W
to Q2W, after three treatment cycles, as implemented in MOR208C203, had no pronounced
effect on PFS based on this analysis.

Exposure-Response Relationship for Clinical Safety
A post-hoc assessment of the DER relationship for safety was performed for clinical trials
MOR208C203/L-MIND and MOR208C201. Overall, there were no obvious trends for an increase
in AESIs or TEAEs in any System Organ Class (SOC) (including the Blood and Lymphatic Disorders
and Cardiac Disorder SOCs) with increasing values for AUC28 or Cmax, as the numbers and
percentages of subjects with such events were equally or randomly distributed across the
4 exposure quartiles. This was also confirmed using logistic regression. For details, refer to
m2.7.4.2.1.5.2.
Lenalidomide
The LEN starting dose was 25 mg daily. This dose was selected on the basis of published data
and the recommended starting dose for patients with myeloma and MCL (REVLIMID USPI,
2019). Published studies indicate a consistent and manageable safety profile for this dose of

the incidence of second primary malignancies with LEN seen in some clinical trials
(REVLIMID USPI, 2019).
The Applicant’s Position:
The dosing regimen of LEN applied in the MOR208C203 (L-MIND) study is consistent with the
USPI in other hemato-oncological indications (REVLIMID USPI, 2019). The duration of LEN
therapy (approximately 12 months) is similar to that in many other combination studies
including LEN in aggressive and indolent lymphoma and is therefore considered appropriate.
The dose and regimen of tafasitamab is supported by exposure-response analysis for efficacy
and safety.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s position that the proposed dosing regimen is supported by
popPK and E-R analyses for efficacy and safety.
6.3.2.3. Is an alternative dosing regimen or management strategy required for
subpopulations based on intrinsic patient factors?
Intrinsic patient factors for tafasitamab exposure were evaluated during population PK analysis.
As determined by stepwise covariate model building, body weight at baseline (WTB) was found
to have a significant effect on CL and V. In addition, covariate effects of albumin (ALB) on
clearance, sex and of the type of disease (NHL/ALL or CLL/SLL) on central volume were also
included in the final Pop-PK model. Covariate effects were moderate in size (5th or 95th

The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s position. The effect of severe renal impairment to end-stage

1.5 x ULN, any AST) is unknown (although no effect is expected).
FCGR (Fc Fragment of IgG Receptor) Polymorphisms and Tumor Response: The Applicant
explored whether germline FCGR2A and FCGR3A polymorphisms affect the response to
tafasitamab (Appendix 19.4). The analyses were limited by small sample size and missing data
and no conclusions could be drawn. More data are required to assess the role of FCGR
polymorphisms on tafasitamab response. The applicant should continue to conduct FCGR
genotyping in ongoing or planned trials.
6.3.2.4. Are there clinically relevant food-drug or drug-drug interactions, and
what is the appropriate management strategy?
No dedicated food-drug or drug-drug interaction studies were performed.
The Applicant’s Position:
As tafasitamab is given as an i.v. infusion, there is no potential for food effects on bioavailability
(BA).
As tafasitamab does not undergo extensive hepatic metabolism and the pharmacological
modes of action do not offer the potential for a direct (anti-)inflammatory activity possibly
modifying Cytochrome P450 function, no specific drug-drug interaction studies were
performed.
Considering the combination of tafasitamab and lenalidomide, the potential impact of
lenalidomide on the PK of tafasitamab was evaluated during covariate analysis of the
population PK analysis (extrinsic factor) with no effect of lenalidomide on tafasitamab PK.
Ɛ >E ŝƐ ƌĞƉŽƌƚĞĚ ƚŽ ďĞ ĞǆĐƌĞƚĞĚ х ϵϬй without being metabolized via renal filtration and is
not subject to any Phase 1 or 2 metabolism, no risk for a potential drug-drug interaction of
tafasitamab on the lenalidomide PK is expected.

The FDA’s Assessment:
Study MOR203 was the pivotal study supporting efficacy of tafasitamab in patients with
relapsed or refractory DLBCL. Studies MORC201 and MORC206 are relevant to the evaluation of
efficacy. The safety evaluation focused on the pivotal trial MOR208C203 supported by a pooled
evaluation of tafasitamab monotherapy in trials MOR208C201, MOR208C202, and XmAb557401.

8 Statistical and Clinical Evaluation
Review of Relevant Individual Trials Used to Support Efficacy
Data supporting the overall efficacy of 12 mg/kg IV tafasitamab in combination with
lenalidomide (LEN) in patients with relapsed or refractory (R/R) diffuse large B-cell lymphoma
(DLBCL) are based on 3 studies, MOR208C203 (L-MIND), MOR208C206 (RE-MIND), and
MOR208C201 (Table 10). MOR208C203 studied the efficacy of tafasitamab in the intended
regimen (in combination with LEN), the proposed dosing regimen, the proposed indication
(adult patients with R/R DLBCL, including DLBCL arising from low grade lymphoma, and who are
(b) (4)
not eligible for,
autologous stem cell transplant) and utilizes the intended commercial
drug formulation. The contribution of each of the single agents in the combination was
determined via 1) MOR208C201, which demonstrated the activity of tafasitamab monotherapy
in DLBCL patients; and 2) MOR208C206, in which the efficacy of LEN monotherapy was
statistically assessed in a matching observational cohort to the combination in MOR208C203
study
MOR208C203, L-MIND
Trial Design
The Applicant’s Description:
This is an ongoing Phase 2, single-arm, open-label, multicenter study conducted at sites in the
80
Version date: July 24, 2019 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)

Reference ID: 4650005

BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation BLA 761163
Monjuvi (tafasitamab-cxix)
U.S. and Europe designed to evaluate the efficacy and safety of tafasitamab + LEN in patients
with R/R DLBCL. The study included patients who had relapsed after or were refractory to at
least 1, but no more than 3 previous systemic regimens (one therapy line must have included a
CD20-targeted therapy such as rituximab [RTX΁), and who were not candidates for HDC and
subsequent ASCT (or relapsed after ASCT). The study design is depicted in Figure 9.
Figure 9: Trial Design of Clinical Study MOR208C203

EOT=end of treatment; FU=follow-up; LEN=lenalidomide; R-R DLBCL=relapsed or refractory
diffuse large B-cell lymphoma; SD=stable disease.
Tafasitamab was administered via IV infusion at a dose of 12.0 mg/kg. For the first 3 cycles
(Cycles 1-3), tafasitamab was administered on Day 1, Day 8, Day 15, and Day 22 of each 28-day
cycle, with an additional loading dose on Day 4 of Cycle 1. Thereafter, tafasitamab was
administered on Day 1 and Day 15 of each 28-day cycle. LEN was self-administered by patients
daily with a starting dose of 25 mg orally on Days 1 to 21 of each 28-day cycle. LEN was
administered in combination with tafasitamab up to 12 cycles in total. In patients with at least
SD after 12 cycles of combination therapy, tafasitamab was administered until disease
progression, unacceptable toxicity or discontinuation for any other reason, whichever came
first.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the above description. Further details of the trial are provided below.

Study Endpoints
The Applicant’s Description:
The primary efficacy endpoint of this study was Best Objective Response Rate (ORR), defined as
the proportion of complete and partial responders (ORR = CR + PR), as assessed by Independent
Radiology/Clinical Review Committee (IRC) according to the International Working Group
revised response criteria for malignant lymphoma (Cheson et al., 2007).
Additional efficacy endpoints included:
x Disease control rate (DCR), defined as the proportion of patients having CR or PR or SD (DCR =
ORR + SD);
x Duration of response (DoR), defined as the time between the initial time point of tumor
response and the first time point where a change in response to progression or relapse was
detailed;
x Progression-free survival (PFS), defined as the time between first study drug dosing and
tumor progression or death from any cause, whichever occurs first;
x Time to progression (TTP), defined as the time from first study drug dosing until time of
progression;
x Overall survival (OS), defined as time from first study drug dosing to the date of death;
x Time to next treatment (TTNT).
86
Version date: July 24, 2019 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)

and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation BLA 761163
Monjuvi (tafasitamab-cxix)
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s description of the study endpoints. The endpoints that FDA
used to determine the benefit of tafa+LEN in the context of the single arm trial are ORR and
DOR. The primary efficacy endpoint, as prespecified, was to be based on an independent,
central radiographic review. Two independent radiologists reviewed the imaging findings with
adjudication of discrepant responses. The disease assessments incorporated review by a clinical
reviewer to determine the overall disease status.

ĂŶ KZZ ŽĨ ϮϬй͘ The populations for analysis were defined as:
x The full analysis set was all patients who received at least one dose of tafasitamab and
one dose of lenalidomide.
x The safety analysis set was all patients who received at least 1 dose of tafasitamab or

having major protocol deviations were to be excluded from the per protocol set.
The primary analysis of the primary efficacy endpoint was based on the full analysis set. To
address the potential for outcome-dependent sampling where eligible patients who initiated
study therapy may be more likely to respond than eligible patients who did not initiate study
therapy, the Agency extended FAS to include one patient who was dosed with tafasitamab but
not lenalidomide, hereafter referred to in this document as the enrolled set. As with FAS, any
patient in the enrolled set without disease assessments are classified as non-responders.

the results. Key changes were as follows:

x

and updates/clarification made to eligibility criteria, patient compliance, and safety
assessments.

determining rituximab refractoriness, and was consistent with the scientific advice
received from regulatory agencies.
Amendment 3 (Oct 23, 2017): The primary change involved extension of tafasitamab
therapy beyond Cycle 24 until disease progression, based on the decision of the treating
investigator.

due to failing an inclusion criterion [PET-positive measurable disease could not be confirmed as
Ă ƉĂƌƚ ŽĨ ƐĐƌĞĞŶŝŶŐ ĂƐƐĞƐƐŵĞŶƚƐ΁͘
The Applicant’s Position:
The most frequently reported protocol violations were those relating to study procedures or
tests. No patient was excluded from the FAS due to a protocol violation. Although a total of 191
protocol deviations occurred, none materially altered the assessment of safety or efficacy.
The FDA’s Assessment:
There was high rate of protocol deviations reported in the L-MIND trial. Of the 81 patients, a
total of 73 patients had at least 1 major protocol deviation. The categories of major protocol
deviations are displayed in Table 12.
An Information Request was sent to the Applicant regarding the high rate of protocol deviations
on the L-MIND trial. The Applicant noted a conservative approach to protocol deviations and a
potential over-reporting of deviations. The majority of the deviations were considered by the
Applicant to not impact the integrity of the trial data. Protocol deviations and violations were
also noted during the remote OSI inspections See Section 4.1. for reviewer comments regarding
protocol deviations. Patients without any post-baseline assessment of DLBCL response or
having major protocol deviations were to be excluded from the per protocol set.

The Applicant’s Position:
Overall, the patient population enrolled in this study was representative of the proposed target
population of adult patients with R/R DLBCL in US, including DLBCL arising from low grade
lymphoma, and who are not eligible for or refuse, ASCT.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The Agency agrees with the Applicants demographic characteristics of the patient population.
However, Table 13 characterized patients in the enrolled set (n=81), not patients in FAS (n=80).
FDA notes that the median age of the patients enrolled ,72 years, is higher than the median age
of patients diagnosed with DLBCL in the US population reflecting the population who are likely
ineligible to receive autologous stem cell transplantation due to age or comorbidities.
FDA does not agree with the statement that the enrolled patients were representative of the
proposed target population of adult patients with R/R DLBCL in the US for the following
reasons: 1) the precise definition (taking into consideration the eligibility criteria and other
comorbidities) of the target population was never specified and 2) there was no random
selection of patients from the target population into the L-MIND trial. For example, the
L-MIND study consisted mostly of Whites. Furthermore, patients in clinical practice may have
more comorbidities that may not be reflected in the L-MIND trial.
The patient population for the MOR208C203 ǁĂƐ ϴϴ͘ϵй ǁŚŝƚĞ͕ Ϯ͘ϱй ƐŝĂŶ͕ ŵŝƐƐŝŶŐ ϳ͘ϰй ĂŶĚ

lack of diversity in the patient population a PMC will be issued to further evaluate the efficacy
and safety of tafasitamab in the underrepresented racial subgroups. Refer to Section 13.

The Applicant’s Position:
Overall, the patient population enrolled in this study was representative of the proposed target
population of adult patients with R/R DLBCL, including DLBCL arising from low grade lymphoma,
and who are not eligible for or refuse, ASCT.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The presentation of baseline disease characteristics in Table 14 is not entirely accurate. As with
Table 13, the results pertain to the enrolled set (n = 81), not FAS (n = 80). With respect to
ĞůĞǀĂƚĞĚ >, ŝŶ ƚŚĞ ĞŶƌŽůůĞĚ ƐĞƚ͕ ϯϲ ;ϰϰйͿ ĚŝĚ ŶŽƚ ŚĂǀĞ ĞůĞǀĂƚĞĚ >, Ăƚ ďĂƐĞůŝŶĞ ǁŚŝůĞ ϰϱ ;ϱϲйͿ
had elevated LDH at baseline. FDA does not agree with the statement that the patients in the LMIND study were representative of the target population as the precise definition of the target
population was never specified and there was no random selection of patients from the target
population into the L-MIND study. Additionally, there were limited representation of other
racial subgroups other than whites.
Although patients with primary refractory disease were enrolled in the L-MIND trial, they
should have been excluded as this was an exclusion criterion. Per the protocol, the
demographics for the primary refractory patient population will not be presented in the USPI.
However, the FDA notes that patients with primary refractory disease have a very poor
prognosis and have limited effective treatment options. The median time since last
discontinuation of last DLBCL treatment or ASCT was 9 months reflects the treatment paradigm
for DLBCL therapies where treatment with combination chemoimmunotherapy is stopped after
a defined number of treatment cycles. The median time to initiation of tafasitamab and
lenalidomide since last progression or relapse was 1.4 months (FDA analysis) which indicates
that the L-MIND trial enrolled a patient population with an immediate need for treatment.
Patients on trial MOR208C203 were enrolled and treated based on local pathology, however,
retrospective central pathology review was done. Based on central pathology a total of 9
patients were non-DLBCL and 2 were unknown. Table 15 lists the patients with confirmed
DLBCL and Table 16 lists the patients who were confirmed with a lymphoma type other than
DLBCL. Patients with a diagnosis other than DLBCL were excluded from the efficacy analysis for
the U.S. Prescribing Information. FDA also notes that DLBCL not otherwise specified was the
predominant subtype in the L-MIND study. In response to an IR, the Applicant provided data
from 9 patients who had a previous diagnosis of indolent lymphoma transformed to DLBCL. The
indication for the U.S. Prescribing Information will reflect the lymphoma subtypes enrolled.

The Applicant’s Position:
Treatment compliance for tafasitamab was ensured as it was infused under the direct
supervision of study personnel. Also for LEN, a high overall compliance to the treatment as
prescribed by the protocol was recorded.
Due to the patient population (primarily elderly with advanced disease), the proportion of
patients taking concomitant medications is expected. There were a few cases of prohibited
concomitant medication usage, the majority of which were associated with corticosteroid
(prednisone) use. Two of these prohibited concomitant medication deviations were considered
major deviations, resulting in patients’ exclusion from the PPS (one patient had concomitant
administration of corticosteroids in excess of a dose specified as per protocol; one patient
received radiotherapy due to disease progression but did not stop study drug as per protocol).
The FDA’s Assessment:
The U.S. Prescribing Information will reflect the use of premedication’s to minimize infusionrelated reactions, especially during the first three infusions.

ϭϴ͘ϱй of patients had primary refractory DLBCL, ϱϬй ŽĨ ƉĂƚients had > two prior lines of

The FDA’s Assessment:
Not all patients in the L-MIND study had confirmed DLBCL at baseline. Out of the 81 patients in
the enrolled set, 71 had confirmed DLBCL, hereafter referred to as the confirmed enrolled set.
During the review, further information was requested from the Applicant regarding the
adjudication of the response determination. As a result of this review by the FDA and the
Applicant, it was determined an error was made as one patient who was previously considered
to be a CR should have been a best response of SD. This will be reflected in the USPI. Among the
71 patients in the confirmed enrolled set, 39 (55й͖ ϵϱй /͗ϰ3й͕ ϲ7й΁Ϳ responded:
x CR. 26 (37й͖ ϵϱй /͗ Ϯ5й͕ 49йͿ
x PR. 13 (1ϴй͖ ϵϱй /͗ ϭϬй͕ ϮϵйͿ
Note that results per FDA’s analyses are similar to those presented by the Applicant. Note also
that 1 of the 71 patients in the confirmed enrolled set was dosed with tafasitamab but not with
lenalidomide. Per the SAP, the primary analysis set was FAS which was defined as consisting of
patients who were dosed with both drugs at least once. This suggests that FDA’s analyses
should include only 70 patients in the denominator. However, as stated earlier, excluding
patients who met eligibility criteria but were not treated is susceptible to outcome-dependent
sampling in the sense that patients not dosed has the potential to be less likely to respond than
those who were dosed. Therefore, FDA’s analysis uses all 71 eligible patients in the
denominator.
A difference in the response rate was noted in patients who had DLBCL confirmed by central
pathology vs patents who had an alternative diagnosis as per central pathology (Figure 13).

Subgroup Analysis
Analyses of ORR and other efficacy parameters were performed for several subpopulations,
(i.e., IPI score, gender, RTX refractoriness, refractoriness to last prior treatment line, primary
refractoriness, prior ASCT, number of prior treatment lines, and age, as well as cell of origin ).
The combination treatment with tafasitamab and LEN had consistent activity in patients
irrespective of their age, or whether primary refractory, refractory to their last prior therapy, or
refractory to RTX.

Applicant’s Position:
For the primary endpoint ORR, consistent results were observed across most patient subgroups
of interest, such as those based on age, gender, primary refractory disease, and refractoriness
to the last prior therapy.
A trend towards a differential effect with overlapping CIs was observed in the subgroups based
on IPI, prior therapy lines, cell of origin. As is typical in DLBCL and many other malignancies,
there was a trend for higher response rates in patients who were less heavily pre-treated.
However, it is difficult to draw robust conclusions from these results, given the small patient
numbers.

FDA agrees with the Applicant that statements about trends in subgroups are difficult to
substantiate given that subgroup sample sizes in the L-MIND study are small, and in general
lead to estimates that are unstable. The following table restricts ORR subgroup results to the 71
patients who were DLBCL-confirmed. FDA notes that the responses were consistent across the

MOR208C206, RE-MIND
Trial Design
The Applicant’s Description:
This observational, retrospective, comparative cohort study MOR208C206 (RE-MIND) was
conducted to generate a matched control cohort for MOR208C203 (L-MIND) to isolate the
contribution of tafasitamab to the combination. The study was designed and conducted to
minimize the potential bias for differences in patient populations, efficacy assessment
techniques, definition of endpoints, source selection, and differences in follow-up time.
Site and patient selection criteria were pre-specified in the study protocol. The same eligibility
criteria as in the MOR208C203 study were applied to this study with respect to the histological
subtypes, number of prior therapy lines, prior therapy types allowed, and ineligibility to receive
ASCT.
Eligibility criteria used in MOR208C203 (L-MIND) but not for MOR208C206 (RE-MIND) were
justified with the following reasons:
1) Inclusion criterion ‘ECOG score 0-2’: ECOG score was expected to be not frequently
documented on patient‘s medical records in routine clinical care.
2) Laboratory parameters, serology and patient’s consent to receive anticoagulation therapy:
Criteria deemed unnecessary in the context of a retrospective data collection. However,
elevated LDH levels, Neutropenia and Anaemia were included as baseline covariates for cohort
balancing.
3) Exclusion criterion ‘patients with primary refractory disease’: The definition of primary
refractory DLBCL was revised during the L-MIND conduct resulting in enrollment and treatment
of 15 patients with primary refractory disease. Consequently, primary refractory disease was
not an exclusion criterion for MOR208C206 and included as baseline covariate for cohort
balancing. Data were collected retrospectively from health records of patients in either real
world, compassionate use and/or clinical trial settings. GCP principles were followed, patient
confidentiality was maintained and the data collected underwent medical review.
The statistical analyses were prospectively defined in the SAP. The effectiveness of LEN
monotherapy was compared with the efficacy outcomes of tafasitamab+LEN combination
therapy in MOR208C203 (L-MIND) after the cohorts were balanced on nine relevant baseline
patient and disease characteristics via 1:1 nearest neighbor matching based on estimated
propensity scores:
104

Of the 490 patients who were enrolled in the observational study, 348 who fulfilled the
eligibility criteria and had a sufficient follow-up (6-months) were included in the full analysis
set for efficacy (FAS). Of these, 218 patients who had a LEN starting dose of 25 mg/day were
included in the FAS25 analysis set. Of those, 140 patients had complete data available on 9
baseline covariates, and hence were used as a patient pool for 1:1 ePS based matching with the
L-MIND cohort. For patients in the L-MIND study the same rules were applied. 5 patients out of
the 81 enrolled patients were excluded from the comparative analysis (reasons: 1 patient did
not fulfill the I/E criteria [had received 4 prior lines of therapy΁, 1 patient was not treated with
lenalidomide and 3 patients discontinued without any post-baseline response assessment or
recorded death).
Thus, 76 patients from each cohort were matched 1:1 and included for analysis of the efficacy
endpoints in the MAS25, the primary analysis set (Figure 15).

BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation BLA 761163
Monjuvi (tafasitamab-cxix)
For a subset of patients in the RE-MIND observational cohort, tumor response assessments
were validated by independent reviewers (1 radiologist and 1 hemato-oncologist) based on
tumor imaging and clinical data in order to evaluate whether response assessments collected in
the observational cohort were accurate representations of patient response. Concordance
between the independent tumor response assessments and the physician-recorded tumor
response assessments in the validation set was measured by percent agreement.
The FDA’s Assessment:
In general, FDA agrees with the Applicant’s description of the design of the RE-MIND
observational study. However, whether the 9 covariates were “relevant” with respect to ORR
heterogeneity and whether they represent a sufficient set in terms of confounder control are
still open questions.
The accuracy of the concordance results from the validation study is unclear given that the
validation set was small. For example, there were only 22 investigator-assessed responders.
Additionally, the concordance results have no direct connection to estimation as the Applicant
did not provide a mechanism for translating the concordance results to the estimation of ORR
based on IRC assessment.
Study Endpoints
The Applicant’s Description:
The primary efficacy endpoint of this study was best overall/objective response rate ORR,
defined as patients with CR or PR as best response achieved at any time between index date
and date of initiation of a new anti-DLBCL medication or death.
Additional efficacy endpoints included;
x
x
x
x
x

DoR, defined as the elapsed time between the date of first documented response (CR or
PR) and the date of event defined as the first documented progression or death (from
any cause).
PFS, defined as the time from the index date to the date of tumor progression or death
from any cause.
OS, defined as the time from the index date until death from any cause (documented by
the date of death).
EFS, defined as the time from the index date to the date of disease progression,
initiation of a new non-study anti-DLBCL medication, or death from any cause, whatever
comes first.
TTNT, defined as the time from index date to the start of next anti-DLBCL therapy
(therapies include- medication, surgery or radiotherapy for any reason including disease
progression, treatment toxicity and patient preference) or death due to any cause,

Disease Control Rate (CR+PR+SD)

Subgroups regarding age, Ann Arbor Stage, refractoriness to last therapy line, number of prior
therapy lines, primary refractoriness, and prior ASCT were evaluated for best ORR, CR, PFS, and
OS.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s description. Due to inherent differences in data elicitation
between clinical trials and clinical practice with respect to what are being measured, how
accurately they are being measured, and when they are measured, FDA considers information
from time-to-event endpoints such as OS, PFS, EFS, and TTNT difficult to interpret and not
relevant to the consideration of the contribution of effect question.
Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments
The Applicant’s Description:
The first objective of this study was to characterize the effectiveness of LEN monotherapy in the
treatment of R/R DLBCL patients. For this purpose, all data of the observational cohort were
summarized using appropriate statistics and tabular overviews. The second objective of this
study was to compare the effectiveness of LEN monotherapy with the efficacy outcomes from
tafasitamab-LEN combination therapy. For this purpose, the following analysis considerations
were applied:
x

x

x
x
x

ePS-based nearest neighbor (NN) 1:1 matching methodology was utilized to balance
cohorts (observational and MOR208C203 cohorts) using the following baseline
covariates to estimate the propensity score: age, Ann Arbor Stage, refractoriness to last
therapy line, number of previous lines of therapy, history of primary refractoriness, prior
ASCT, neutropenia, anemia, and elevated lactate dehydrogenase (LDH).
Various analysis populations for the comparative analysis were defined based on the
patient’s follow-up time, LEN starting dose, and cohort balancing approach to address
potential selection bias. The primary analysis set was the Nearest Neighbour (NN) 1:1
Matched Analysis Set of patients with a LEN starting dose of 25mg/day (MAS25).
The primary endpoint, ORR, was estimated using logistic regression. The resulting odds
ƌĂƚŝŽ ĂŶĚ ŝƚƐ ĂƐƐŽĐŝĂƚĞĚ ϵϱй / ǁĞƌĞ ĞƐƚŝŵĂƚĞĚ͘ dŚĞ ƉƌŝŵĂƌǇ ĂŶĂůǇƐŝƐ ǁĂƐ ďĂƐĞĚ ŽŶ
Fisher’s exact test and was performed on the MAS25.
A sensitivity analysis was performed to achieve a higher degree of balance in a subgroup
of patients by applying a caliper to the 1:1 NN matching algorithm resulting in 2 x 68
patients in the MAS25_Cal analysis population.
McNemar’s conditional test for paired data and conditional logistic regression were
conducted as sensitivity analyses for ORR, with p-values reported associated with the

treatment cohort effect. All time-to-event endpoints were analyzed using standard
Kaplan-Meier methodology.
A sensitivity analysis was performed based on a Balancing Overlap Weights approach to
balance cohorts using only the FAS and FAS with starting dose 25 mg of LEN
monotherapy (FAS25).
Subgroup analyses were performed determining ORR, OS and PFS in the respective
subgroups.
An inclusion of approximately 500 patients in the observational cohort was expected to
be necessary to complete a successful NN 1:1 matching for cohort balancing before
conducting comparative efficacy outcome analyses. As the MOR208C203 ITT cohort
consisted of n=81 patients, the ePS-based 1:1 matching resulted in a sample size of
n=162 at maximum for the primary analysis method.

The SAP was amended once, on October 29, 2019. Changes since the original SAP dated
September 09, 2019 addressed FDA comments, and included clarifications, and added details
for statistical programming. There were no changes to the concept of cohort balancing or
primary analysis methodology.
The FDA’s Assessment:
In general, FDA agrees with the Applicant’s description.
