Balance between the 2 treatment cohorts:
The standardized mean difference (SMD) is a measure of balance achieved for the proportions
of a certain dichotomized baseline characteristic.
Of the 9 covariates used for NN 1:1 matching (age, Ann Arbor Stage, refractoriness to last
therapy line, number of previous lines of therapy, history of primary refractoriness, prior ASCT,
neutropenia, anemia, and elevated LDH), 5 achieved a SMD <0.1, 2 achieved a SMD <0.2, and 2
ĂĐŚŝĞǀĞĚ Ă ^D ŽĨ чϬ͘Ϯϵ ;ŶŶ ƌďŽƌ ĂŶĚ EƵŵďĞƌ ŽĨ WƌŝŽƌ >ŝŶĞƐͿ ŝŶ ƚŚĞ D^Ϯϱ. For each of the 9
ďĂƐĞůŝŶĞ ĐŽǀĂƌŝĂƚĞƐ͕ ĂŶ ^D ŽĨ чϬ͘ϭϴ was achieved for MAS25_Cal.
The Applicant’s Position:
The comparative cohorts are representative for patients with R/R DLBCL and have baseline
characteristics representative of the target population. The two cohorts were well matched
with a SMD <0.20 for 7 of the 9 covariates in the MAS25, which allows for a robust estimation
of the additional treatment effect attributable to tafasitamab when added to LEN as in the
MOR208C203 trial. Sensitivity analysis achieved a higher degree of balance with an SMD of
чϬ͘ϭϴ ĨŽƌ Ăůů ϵ ďĂƐĞůŝŶĞ ĐŽǀĂƌŝĂƚĞƐ in the MAS25_Cal analysis population.

Table 23. Below (Table 24) we restrict patients to those that were DLBCL-confirmed, which
affects the tafa+LEN cohort. NOTE: Statements about two cohorts being balanced in the setting
of the RE-MIND study is difficult to substantiate. From a marginal distribution point of view,
the data show that LEN patients exhibit poorer Ann Arbor, ECOG, and number of prior lines.
Additionally, even if the marginal distributions of these characteristics look similar, whether
visually or by the SMD metric, it does not in any way guarantee that the joint distributions of
multiple risk factors are similar. Unlike randomization which theoretically induces similar
marginal and joint distributions of patient characteristics, the RE-MIND observational study
cannot guarantee this. An assessment of the joint distribution cannot be rigorously performed
as the sample size is too small to identify the joint distributions in a robust manner. As shown in
Table 24, an estimate of the joint distribution of relapse to last line and number of prior lines
suggest that jointly the baseline disease characteristics between the two cohorts may be
imbalanced.

Sensitivity analysis performed with the MAS25_Cal set achieved a standardized difference of
чϬ͘ϭϴ ĨŽƌ Ăůů ϵ ďĂƐĞůŝŶĞ ĐŽǀĂƌŝĂƚĞƐ ĂŶĚ ůĞĚ ƚŽ ŚŝŐŚůǇ ĐŽŶƐŝƐƚĞŶƚ ƌĞƐƵůƚƐ ǁŝƚŚ ƚŚĞ ƉƌŝŵĂƌǇ ĂŶĂůǇƐŝƐ͘
Additional sensitivity analyses were performed and confirmed the primary analysis. These
included using the assumption of correlated data for matched datasets and performing Mc
Nemar’s instead of Fisher’s exact test, analysis of PFS and EFS with different censoring rules,
analyses of ORR, PFS and OS with cohorts balanced using overlap weights constructed from the
ePS (i.e., instead of 1:1 NN matching), multiple imputation on missing baseline covariates and
modified analysis sets including patients not satisfying the 6-month follow up rule.
The Applicant’s Position:
Combination tafasitamab and LEN demonstrated substantial evidence of efficacy over LEN
monotherapy cohort in R/R DLBCL for the MAS25 set, with ORR of ϲϳ͘ϭй ǀƐ ϯϰ͘Ϯй ŝŶ ƚŚĞ
tafasitamab + LEN cohort vs LEN monotherapy cohorts, respectively. The difference of almost
doubling the ORR and tripling the CR rate between the two cohorts is clinically meaningful.
These results were confirmed by all sensitivity analyses. The addition of tafasitamab to LEN
results in increased activity in R/R DLBCL, demonstrating the contribution of tafasitamab to the
combination regimen. Notably, outcomes from the observational cohort of LEN monotherapy

were similar to the published literature with LEN monotherapy in R/R DLBCL with best ORR

The FDA’s Assessment:
In general, FDA agrees with the Applicant’s presentation in Table 25. However, due to the
nature of the observational study, statements about evidence of substantial advantage of
tafa+LEN over LEN monotherapy cannot be rigorously substantiated. Table 26 following
provides additional FDA-generated results restricted to centrally-confirmed DLBCL, per IRC.
NOTE: In the LEN arm, only patients in the validation study had IRC response. For those not in
the validation study, they did not have IRC response; thus, the response for these patients are
INV-assessed.

FDA’s results are similar to the Applicant’s results. However, the interpretation remains
ambiguous. The primary reasons stem from these sources. First, as noted previously, the
distribution (marginal and joint) of the observable covariates, are not well-balanced. Second,
the role of unmeasured confounders are unknown. Thus, how much of the observed difference
in the results is due in part or in whole to factors favorable to the tafa+LEN arm is still unclear.
Third, since not all patients in the LEN cohort had IRC-assessed response, the potential for
outcome misclassification exists.

time-to-event endpoints supported the primary analysis results, with clinically meaningful and
statistically significant differences between tafasitamab + LEN combination and LEN
monotherapy.
The FDA’s Assessment:
Due to the limitations of the observational study, the secondary endpoints described by the
Applicant are not utilized.

Additional Analyses Conducted on the Individual Trial
Physician-reported, real-world tumor response assessments were validated in a subset of
patients via independent radiology and clinical review assessments in the retrospective,
observational cohort of R/R DLBCL patients treated with LEN monotherapy in the RE-MIND
study. The combined concordance for the responders (CR+PR) + non-responders (SD+PD) was
7ϵ͘ϴй in a subset of 79 patients assessed.
The Applicant’s Position:
The validation assessment demonstrated that response assessment by an independent panel of
experts was generally concordant with the response assessment by the treating physicians.
The FDA’s Assessment:
Based on the Applicant’s definition of concordance, FDA agrees with the Applicant’s statement.
However, as noted earlier, there were only 22 patients who were responders per INV. As such,
it is not clear how reliable the concordance estimates are. In addition, it is not clear what the
concordance threshold should be for the purpose of deciding whether the results are robust to
misclassification.
MOR208C201
Trial Design
The Applicant’s Description:
This trial was a phase 2a, 2-stage, open-label, single-arm, multicenter, study to evaluate the
efficacy and safety of tafasitamab monotherapy in adult patients with R/R NHL who had
received at least 1 prior therapy containing RTX.

120
Version date: July 24, 2019 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the Applicant
and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 4650005

BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation BLA 761163
Monjuvi (tafasitamab-cxix)
A two-stage design was used to minimize exposure of patients with NHL subtypes not
adequately responding to MOR208. In stage 1, ten patients were to be enrolled into each of
four NHL subtype-specific cohorts; DLBCL, FL, other iNHL and MCL. If >Ϯ ƉĂƚŝĞŶƚƐ ;ϮϬйͿ ŝŶ Ă
cohort had a partial or complete response after 8–12 weeks of treatment, then >20 additional
patients with that NHL subtype were to be enrolled. Based on the data from stage 1, the DLBCL
and FL subtype-specific cohorts were expanded in Stage 2. At the completion of enrolment
(n=92), the subtype-specific cohorts included following number of patients: DLBCL n=35; FL
n=34; other iNHL n=11; MCL n=12.
Treatment consisted of tafasitamab monotherapy which was to be administered until 8 weeks
(2 cycles) for all patients. Patients who had a response of at least SD or better, could receive on
additional treatment cycle. At the end of cycle 3, patients who had a PR or CR, (regardless of
Stage 1 or 2 or the histological subtype) were eligible to receive further study drug until
progression (either every two or every four weeks, as decided by the investigator).
The FDA’s Assessment:
As mentioned by the Applicant the primary objective of this trial was to assess the antitumor
activity of single-agent tafasitamab in patients with relapsed or refractory NHL. The dose used
was the same as the pivotal trial, 12 mg/kg. Patients were treated for an initial 2 cycles, each 28
days with tafasitamab administrated on day 1, 8, 15 and 22. Premedication was given for the
first 3 infusions. Patients with stable disease would then qualify for cycle 3, again tafasitamab
administrated on day 1, 8, 15 and 22. For patients with an ongoing response of at least a PR at
the end of cycle 3, may then progression to receive maintenance treatment which was further
treatment every 2 weeks or monthly until disease progression.
Premedication consisted of antipyretics, histamine H1 receptor blockers, and
glucocorticosteroids, which were given for the first 3 infusions.
The secondary objectives for this trial included evaluation of the duration of response, safety
and tolerability, evaluation of potential immunogenicity and evaluation of the
pharmacokinetics and pharmacodynamics in patients with relapsed or refractory NHL.
Design
The trial enrolled in two stages. Stage 1 consisted of at least 10 patients per NHL subtype:
x FL
x DLBCL
x MCL
x Other indolent NHL
For those NHL subtypes with 2 or more responders, an additional 20 patients will be enrolled
for a total of 30 patients per subtype for stage 2. The cohorts may have been stopped for

Inclusion criteria (summarized)
x Age ш18 years of age
x Histologically confirmed diagnosis according to the Revised European American
lymphoma/World Health Organization classification:
o Follicular lymphoma
o Other indolent NHL
o DLBCL
o MCL
x Progression of lymphoma after at least 1 prior rituximab containing regimen. This could
be 1 completed cycle of combination therapy with rituximab and chemotherapy or at
least 4 weekly administration of rituximab as monotherapy.
x If previous ASCT, patients must be at least 4 weeks following transplantation and have
full hematologic recovery
x Life expectancy of >3 months
x ECOG performance status of < 3
x The following laboratory criteria at screening:
o ANC ш 1.0 (1000/mm3)
o Platelet count ш75 x 109/L
o Hemoglobin ш8.0 g/dL
o Serum creatinine < 2.0 x ULN
o Total bilirubin ч2.0 x ULN
o ALT and AST ч 2.5 x ULN
x Females of childbearing potential, a negative pregnancy test and use of a double-barrier
contraception during and for 3 months after the last dose or documented total
hysterectomy and/or oophorectomy, tubal ligation.
x If male, an effective barrier method must be used during the trial and for 3 months after
the last dose if sexually active with a female of childbearing potential.
Exclusion Criteria (summarized)
x Previous treatment with an anti-CD19 antibody
x Previous allogeneic stem cell transplantation
x Clinically significant cardiovascular disease or cardiac insufficiency
x Positive hepatitis serology
x History of HIV infection
x Any active systemic infection requiring active parenteral antibiotic therapy within 4
weeks
x Current treatment with immunosuppressive agents other than corticosteroids, not more
than 10 mg prednisone or equivalent.

Management of Toxicities
If a patient developed a study drug related grade 3 or 4 toxicity and the toxicity had not
resolved to grade 1, or baseline if pre-existing, at the day of the next scheduled dose, treatment
was to be postponed. If treatment was postponed for more than 15 days for the same toxicity,
the patients treatment was to be discontinued.
Patients were to begin the next treatment cycle of criteria were met. These criteria are below:
x ANC ш 1.0 x 109/L
x Platelet count ш75 x 109/L
x Hemoglobin ш 8.0 g/dL (may be transfused)
x Serum creatinine < 2.0 x ULN
x Total bilirubin ч 2.0 x ULN
x ALT and ASLT ч 2.5 x ULN
x No treatment related grade 3 or 4 toxicity in CNS, cardiovascular, infections or
gastrointestinal system organ class.
Prophylactic antibiotics/antifungals were allowed at the discretion of the investigator.
Medications for treatment of symptoms of NHL or other diseases were allowed at the
discretion of the investigator.
Study Endpoints
The Applicant’s Description:
The primary efficacy endpoint was best ORR (CR + PR), assessed by Investigators. Additional
efficacy endpoints included;
x
x
x
x

TTP, the time from study entry (first dosing) until documented lymphoma progression or
death as a result of lymphoma. Deaths from other causes were censored at the time of
death.
DoR, defined as the time from first meeting of criteria for response (i.e., CR or PR) to
first documentation of relapse or progression.
PFS, defined as the time from study entry (first dosing) until lymphoma progression or
death as a result of any cause.
SD rate, defined as failure to attain CR/PR or PD.

The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s description of the endpoints. Response was determined using
the revised IWG response criteria for malignant lymphoma (Cheson response criteria), standard
criteria for response assessment in DLBCL. TTP is considered to be exploratory. Stable disease is
not considered clinically meaningful from a regulatory perspective. Since this is a single arm
study, time-to event endpoints such as PFS and OS have limited interpretability.

Protocol Amendments
The Applicant’s Description:
The initial protocol was amended four times prior to the completion of the study. These
changes had no impact on the integrity of the trial or interpretation of the results. Key changes
were as follows:
x

x

x

Amendment 1 (Date: January 07, 2013), submitted prior to patient enrollment, included
changes to various safety assessments and reporting, as well as eliminated minimal
disease assessment, removed a longitudinal assessment of CD19 expression on tumor
cells, and introduced formal study termination rules.
Amendment 2 (Date: October 02, 2013) was done after patient enrollment began. It
allowed for patients who had an ongoing response of at least PR after Cycle 3 eligible to
receive 12 mg/kg tafasitamab as extended therapy until disease progression; CT/PET
examinations performed up to 3 weeks before Cycle 1 Day 1 could be used as a patient’s
baseline assessment; the futility decision if a further 20 patients in a subtype would be
enrolled to continue enrollment after the first 10 patients in each subtype could now be
based on response at both 8 and 12 weeks (since therapy with monoclonal antibodies is
expected to have a slower effect than cytotoxic drugs); and radiographic investigations
could now be performed 4 working days before or after the actual visit. Clarifications to
CT/PET procedures, dosing/body weight calculations, lymphomas that could be
included in the subtype of indolent lymphoma, concomitant medication entry into the
eCRF were added.
Amendment 3 (Date: April 15, 2014) introduced minor changes to various safety
assessments; added a definition for having received at least 1 prior therapy containing
RTX; updated eligibility and management of patients with positive HBV screens;
excluded allergic reactions and infusion-related reactions grade 2 and less from the
definition of AEs of special interest; widened the visit window (due to practical reasons)
for radiographic assessment by 3 days in treatment period; added an inclusion criteria
for transformed lymphomas that the subtype at screening for the MOR208C201 study
was relevant for the assignment to the respective subtype (this ensured a correct
classification of patients who had their DLBCL transformed from a prior indolent
lymphoma to the DLBCL subtype cohort in the study); widened the window for
postponements of visits; detailed the use of MRI; clarified that early termination of
patients was defined as patients who terminated the study before the end of Cycle 2;
and clarified that patients who terminated before the end of Cycle 2 would undergo an
EOS visit, whereas patients who had at least SD at the end of Cycle 2 and had not
progressed would continue with the first follow up visit.

performed, SAE not reported within timeframe, CT images not performed with contrast as
required, vital signs collected out of allowed timeframes, radiological evaluation not reported
within timeframe, or biopsy at Screening not performed correctly). A further 21.7й ƉĂƚŝĞŶƚƐ
had protocol deviations due to procedural errors during the study drug administration (e.g.
ŝŶĐŽƌƌĞĐƚ ŝŶĨƵƐŝŽŶ ƌĂƚĞƐ Žƌ ŝŶĨƵƐŝŽŶ ŵĂƚĞƌŝĂůƐ ΀ůŝŶĞƐ ĂŶĚ ĨŝůƚĞƌƐ΁ than as specified per protocol
were used).
The Applicant’s Position:
After a critical review of these protocol deviations, the applicant concluded that the analysis
and the overall interpretation of efficacy and safety results were not impacted. A Per Protocol
analysis Set (PPS) was not prespecified in the study protocol.
The FDA’s Assessment:
While it is not clear what is meant by the phrase “efficacy and safety results were not
impacted”, FDA considers the efficacy and safety results of tafasitamab monotherapy to be
interpretable within the context of protocol deviations.

The Applicant’s Position:
The DLBCL patients enrolled in this study were representative of the intended population in the
BLA (R/R DLBCL, not eligible for ASCT). Their baseline characteristics were similar to the patients
enrolled in the pivotal L-MIND study. Thus, this study provides an appropriate population to
characterize the efficacy of tafasitamab monotherapy in R/R DLBCL patients.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The size of the DLBCL cohort is small such that a characterization of its representativeness of
the “intended population” is difficult to substantiate.

One patient in the FL subgroup received concomitant therapy excluded per protocol.

Treatment compliance for tafasitamab was ensured as it was infused under the direct
supervision of study personnel. No statistical analysis for measurement of treatment
compliance was planned for this study. The patient who received the excluded concomitant
therapy did not change the interpretation of study outcomes.
The FDA’s Assessment: The FDA did not independently confirm the Applicant position above,
but note the position stated above stated by the Applicant.

Efficacy Results – Secondary and other relevant endpoints
Based on the Investigator evaluation, the median DoR was 20.1 months for the DLBCL
subgroup. Of the 9 responders, five patients were in remission for >12 months and 2 were in
remission for >4 years. The 12-ŵŽŶƚŚ W&^ ƌĂƚĞ ǁĂƐ ϯϰ͘ϯй ĨŽƌ >>, and the median TTP was

The Applicant’s Position:
The data from the DLBCL subgroup demonstrate a long duration of response with tafasitamab
monotherapy. All time-to-event endpoints supported the primary analysis results. No statistical
inference for the time-to-event endpoints can be made from this single-arm study, and results
presented are descriptive only.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Sponsor’s general assessment. The phrase “demonstrate a long duration of
response” is not statistically defensible as there were only 9 patients in the denominator.

The analysis of subgroups based on number of prior treatment lines, IPI, age, or refractoriness

Results from subgroup analysis are difficult to substantiate given that subgroup sample sizes
are small, and in general lead to estimates that are unstable.

The Applicant’s Position:
In study MOR208C201, tafasitamab monotherapy showed activity in DLBCL patients. Study
MOR208C203 demonstrated a strongly increased signal of efficacy with the combination
regimen of tafasitamab + LEN combination therapy. The study was single armed, and the
superiority of the tafasitamab + LEN combination over LEN monotherapy could not be
statistically tested. Cross-study comparisons with published LEN monotherapy results are
difficult to interpret given potential differences in the patient population for key baseline
characteristics. Therefore, MOR208C206 was conducted to generate a control cohort of LEN
monotherapy for comparison to the combination regimen. The prospectively designed
ePS-based 1:1 matching in this study allowed for a robust estimation of the additional
treatment effect attributable to tafasitamab when added to LEN, as in Study MOR208C203.
Compared with LEN monotherapy, the tafasitamab + LEN combination resulted in a ORR that
ǁĂƐ ŚŝŐŚĞƌ ďǇ ϯϯй͕ nearly two-fold the ORR observed for LEN monotherapy. Further, the CR
rate was three-fold higher in the combination arm versus LEN monotherapy, suggesting

132
Version date: July 24, 2019 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)

and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 4650005

BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation BLA 761163
Monjuvi (tafasitamab-cxix)
substantial additional activity for the novel combination of tafasitamab + LEN versus LEN
monotherapy in transplant ineligible R/R DLBCL patients.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA’s results for the L-MIND study are as described earlier and do not numerically agree with
those in Table 28 above. As noted previously, FDA’s analyses were restricted to patients who
were DLBCL-confirmed. For the RE-MIND study, this reduces the denominator to 66 patients
and the numerator to 39, resulting in ORZ ŽĨ ϱϵй ;ϵϱй /͗ ϰϲй͕ ϳϭйͿ͘ While the data suggest
that tafa+LEN provides for a substantial improvement over LEN with respect to ORR, it is
unknown how much of that improvement is due to confounding factors. While the RE-MIND
study attempts to provide clarity, whether the study is robust in the sense of adequately
controlling for confounding remains an open question.

MOR208C206, and MOR208C201, as well as for 2 published prospective LEN monotherapy trials
(Czuczman et al, 2007; Witzig et al, 2011).

The Applicant’s Position:
Deeper, more durable responses were observed for the combination arm of tafasitamab + LEN
versus LEN monotherapy. DoR results above show that patients who achieve a CR with
tafasitamab + LEN combination therapy have a high probability to remain in remission for an
extended period of time. Patients achieving a PR as best response tend to progress within the
next 6 months. Median DoR was extended by approximately 14 months in the combination
tafasitamab+LEN as compared to LEN monotherapy, suggesting substantial additional activity
for the combination therapy as opposed to LEN alone.
The observed DoR, PFS, OS, TTP, and DCR, with tafasitamab supported the meaningful clinical
benefit of treatment with tafasitamab 12 mg/kg alone or in combination with LEN and
represent a substantial improvement in a population of patients with a high unmet medical
need.
The FDA’s Assessment:
As noted earlier, FDA does not consider time-to-event endpoints relevant to the determination
of efficacy outside of the randomized setting. However, FDA notes that the durability of

response in the responders is approximately 2 years.
Subpopulations
Data for exploratory subgroup analyses is presented in each of the individual trial sections.
The Applicant’s Position:
The primary endpoint ORR was consistent across all presented subgroups (i.e., age group, IPI
score, number of prior treatment lines, primary refractoriness, refractoriness to last prior
treatment line, prior ASCT, and NK cell count at baseline).
Subgroups analyses did not identify any major demographic factor or baseline disease
characteristic predictive of lack of response to the tafasitamab + LEN combination or
tafasitamab monotherapy in any of the trials.
The FDA’s Assessment:
Results from subgroup analysis are difficult to substantiate given that subgroup sample sizes
are small, and in general lead to estimates that are unstable.
Additional Efficacy Considerations
The FDA’s Assessment:
As noted in previous sections, the RE-MIND observational study was designed to learn about
the activity of tafa+LEN relative to LEN alone. However, there were a number of issues that
limited the interpretability of results. The Statistical Issues subsection in SUMMARY AND
CONCLUSIONS provides additional details.
Compliance with Good Clinical Practices
The Applicant’s Position:
All studies included in the development program for tafasitamab in DLBCL have been conducted
in compliance with Good Clinical Practice and with the approvals of the relevant Ethics
Committees or Institutional Review Boards. Informed consent was obtained for all patients, and
the studies were performed in accordance with the version of the Declaration of Helsinki that
applied at the time the studies were conducted. Where required, regulatory approval was
obtained from the relevant health authority.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s description.

21 CFR Part 54. None of the investigators or sub-investigators involved in the clinical trials had

For MOR208C203, information was missing from 3 sub-investigators at 3 distinct
locations, none of whom have made a significant contribution to the data due to no
patients being treated at the site during the time the concerned sub-investigator was
assigned to the study (2 cases), or a limited amount of time spent as a sub-investigator
for that site (1 case; 1 month).
For MOR208C201, information was missing from 1 sub-investigator who was assigned to
the study 2 months after the last patient completed the study at the concerned site.
Thus, the sub-investigator could not have made a significant contribution to the data.
For Xmab5574-01, information was missing from 2 sub-investigators at 1 study center
due to unresponsiveness or lack of contact information.

The Applicant’s Position:
The lack of disclosures across investigators in the clinical trials does not raise concerns about
the overall integrity of study data. The 3 studies which formed the basis for the demonstration
of efficacy (MOR28C203, MOR208C201 and MOR208C206) were conducted over multiple sites
and in multiple countries, thus limiting the potential for a single investigator or sub-investigator
to make a significant contribution to the data.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s description of the financial disclosures on for the L-MIND trial.

For applicable studies, study monitoring was performed in accordance with ICH E6 GCP

written instructions, the protocol, and all applicable laws and regulations. The handling of data,
including data quality control, complied with all applicable regulatory guidelines.
The Applicant’s Position:
No meaningful concerns are anticipated in the quality and integrity of the submitted datasets;
these were sufficiently complete to allow for a thorough review of efficacy. Furthermore, no
data integrity concerns were reported following completion of site inspections by the Applicant.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The datasets, Clinical Study Reports, narratives and responses to information requests were
considered adequate to allow for review of the submission.

ĂŶĚ K^ ŽĨ ϳϯ͘ϳй ;ϵϱй /͗ ϲϮ͘Ϯ͕ ϴϮ͘ϮͿ͕ ϲϯ͘ϲй ;ϵϱй /͗ ϱϬ͘ϵ͕ ϳϯ͘ϳͿ͕ ĂŶĚ ϱϲ͘ϲ ;ϵϱй /͗ ϰϯ͘Ϭ͕ ϲϴ͘ϬͿ Ăƚ
12, 18, and 24 months, respectively. Secondary endpoint analyses were generally consistent
with the primary analysis, and consistent results were observed across patient subgroups of
interest.
The contribution of each of the single agents (tafasitamab and LEN) in the combination was
determined via 1) the MOR208C201 clinical trial for tafasitamab, which demonstrated the
activity of tafasitamab monotherapy in DLBCL patients, and 2) the MOR208C206 (RE-MIND)
study, in which the efficacy of LEN monotherapy was assessed in a matching observational
cohort to the MOR208C203 (L-MIND) study. The MOR208C206 (RE-MIND) study provides a
statistical comparison of LEN monotherapy vs. tafasitamab + LEN.
In summary, the effect of the tafasitamab and LEN combination therapy can be considered to
lead to substantial additional activity versus each agent alone, since for physician-assessed best
ORR, CR and median PFS, the effect of the combination is greater than the sum of the
respective monotherapies when considering the results in the MOR208C206 (RE-MIND) trial for
LEN and the results in study MOR208C201 for tafasitamab.
Further, in the MOR208C206 (RE-MIND study), the results reached statistical significance and all
time to event endpoints supported the primary analysis results, with clinically meaningful and
statistically significant differences between tafasitamab + LEN combination and LEN
monotherapy.
The aggregate patient population was similar for demographic and baseline characteristics.
Patients were elderly and had DLBCL, an orphan indication. Thus, it is reasonable and valid to
draw conclusions about the efficacy of the tafasitamab + LEN combination from this patient
population.
The Applicant’s Position:
Patient outcomes with available treatments for R/R DLBCL remain largely unsatisfactory, in
particular for patients unable to receive high-dose chemotherapy followed by ASCT or
treatment with chimeric antigen receptor T cell therapies. Efficacy in this application is based
on IRC-assessed ORR for the combination of tafasitamab and LEN, for which a clinically
meaningful ORR of ϲϬй ĂŶĚ Z ŽĨ 42.5й were observed. The responses observed with the
137
Version date: July 24, 2019 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the Applicant
and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 4650005

BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation BLA 761163
Monjuvi (tafasitamab-cxix)
tafasitamab and LEN were durable, in particular in patients who achieved a CR. The
contribution of tafasitamab to the combination was put into context by the matched
observational cohort of LEN-treated patients, as the combination demonstrated a nearly twofold increase in ORR and three-fold increase in CR over LEN monotherapy.
Similar, statistically significant benefit was observed in terms of duration of response and
overall survival for patients who received tafasitamab + LEN vs LEN alone.
Overall, the data presented in this application demonstrates substantial evidence of
effectiveness for tafasitamab in combination with LEN to treat patients with R/R DLBCL who are
not suitable for HDC and ASCT. Furthermore, the effectiveness of this regimen compares
favorably with that of LEN alone in the MOR208C206 (RE-MIND) study and also compares
favorably in the context of many other salvage regimens evaluated in this setting.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant that patients with relapsed or refractory DLBCL who are unable
to receive high-dose chemotherapy followed by ASCT have limited treatment options. The
efficacy data from the L-MIND study with supportive evidence of the response rate of single
agent tafasitamab from the relapsed or refractory DLBCL population from the single arm
MOR208C201 Study represents a clinically meaningful treatment benefit of tafasitamab in this
patient population. FDA’s analysis of efficacy from the L-MIND trial was based on 71 patients
with central pathology confirmed diagnosis of DLBCL and are different from that stated by the
Applicant. FDA disagrees with the Applicant’s assertions regarding the statistical significance of
the results from the RE-MIND study. FDA notes several limitations with the RE-MIND study and
does not agree that the data can be used to confirm statistically significant improvement of
response of tafa+LEN compared to lenalidomide. The response data from this study was used to
provide context to the results from the L-MIND trial. FDA notes that the response rate of single
agent lenalidomide from the RE-MIND study is consistent with the data from the literature.
The time-to-event endpoints (PFS and OS) are difficult to interpret in single-arm studies. The
time-to-event endpoints are not likely to constitute supportive evidence because their
measurements in isolation are out of context and comparing them to historical controls is
prone to bias. FDA also notes that the eligibility criteria for L-MIND trial did not specify a
requirement for ineligibility for CAR-T therapy.
See also the SUMMARY AND CONCLUSIONS section.

Assessment of the safety of tafasitamab is based on data from 569 patients in seven studies,
analyzed as follows:

The Applicant’s Position:

The total safety data available allow for a comprehensive assessment of the safety profile of
tafasitamab, at a dose of 12mg/kg, in combination with lenalidomide followed by tafasitamab
monotherapy and an evaluation of the overall benefit-risk, in adult patients with relapsed or
refractory diffuse large B-cell lymphoma (DLBCL), including DLBCL arising from low grade
lymphoma, who are not eligible for, or refuse autologous stem cell transplant (ASCT). This safety
population is considered adequate for the detection and characterization of common AEs and to
provide guidance on toxicity management.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The safety review primarily focused on the pivotal trial, MOR208C203 in patients with DBLCL. In
addition to the pivotal trial, the safety of the three monotherapy trials, XmAb5574-01,
MOR208C201 and MOR208C202 was evaluated.

Monjuvi (tafasitamab-cxix)
Safety Review Approach
To demonstrate the safety of tafasitamab in combination with lenalidomide followed by
tafasitamab monotherapy, the primary analysis included detailed summaries of the clinical
safety data from the pivotal trial MOR208C203 in 81 patients with DLBCL, of whom 80 received
at least one dose each of tafasitamab and lenalidomide, which were evaluated to characterize
common AEs and to provide guidance on toxicity management. In order to understand the
relative contributions of each component to the safety profile of the combination, in addition
to the analysis of safety data from the tafasitamab monotherapy phase of MOR208C203, a
pooled analysis of data from a total of 141 patients in three tafasitamab monotherapy trials
(XmAb5574-01, MOR208C201, and MOR208C202) was performed. Of these, MOR208C201, in
NHL, included 35 patients with the DLBCL sub-type.
A comparison of the safety data from the combination phase of MOR208C203 with safety data
for lenalidomide monotherapy in the lenalidomide USPI was also performed.
This primary analysis was supported by a review of deaths and SAEs from the patients in the
three trials of tafasitamab in combination with other therapies: MOR208C205 (COSMOS),
MOR208C204 (B-MIND), and OSU-13031 trials (see Table 10).
The Applicant’s Position:
The pooling strategy was agreed with FDA as part of a Type B pre-BLA meeting (July 01, 2019).
This approach enables characterization of the risk profile for tafasitamab in combination with
lenalidomide followed by monotherapy in the target population.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA safety evaluation focused primarily on the pivotal trial MOR208C203. The safety of the
pooled analysis of data from the three monotherapy trials was also evaluated.
Review of the Safety Database
Overall Exposure
The Safety Analysis Set was defined as all patients who received at least one dose of study drug.
For studies (XmAb5574-01, MOR208C201, and MOR208C202), tafasitamab was the only study
drug. For the L-MIND study, tafasitamab and lenalidomide were the study drugs. Patients were
analyzed as treated. The primary safety analysis for this submission includes 81 patients (80 of
whom received both tafasitamab and lenalidomide, and all of whom received tafasitamab) in
study MOR208C203 and 141 patients from the tafasitamab monotherapy studies.
In MOR208C203, the median time on tafasitamab in combination with lenalidomide was
190.0 days (range: 3 to 436 days) (Table 30).

Adequacy of the safety database

An integrated safety database was created to support the integrated safety analyses described
in this application. Integrated Clinical Data Interchange Standards Consortium (CDISC) Analysis
Data Model (ADaM) datasets were created from individual study ADaM or SDTM datasets and
followed the CDISC Analysis Data Model Implementation Guide version 1.1. A process of data
reconciliation ensured that data on the safety database were consistent with those on the
clinical trials database. The datalock date for the safety analyses was 30th June 2019.

Monjuvi (tafasitamab-cxix)
The evaluation of safety is based on the primary safety analysis comprising data from the
81 patients in the pivotal MOR208C203 study, and the 141 patients in the three tafasitamab
monotherapy studies as described in Section 8.2.4
The Applicant’s Position:
The safety database is sufficient to evaluate the safety of the combination of tafasitamab with
lenalidomide followed by tafasitamab monotherapy in the target population.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The clinical reviewer agrees the safety database was adequate.
Adequacy of Applicant’s Clinical Safety Assessments
Issues Regarding Data Integrity and Submission Quality
All individual case safety reports were followed up as necessary to obtain complete information
on the case. A process of source data verification during study monitoring ensured the accuracy
of these data. A thorough QC check was performed on all data entered to the safety database,
and data were reconciled with the clinical trials database. No data integrity concerns were
reported from site audits.
The Applicant’s Position:
No meaningful concerns were detected in the quality and integrity of the submitted datasets or
individual case narratives; these are sufficiently complete to allow for a thorough review of
safety.
The FDA’s Assessment:
Based on the datasets, narratives, Clinical Study Reports, and other
submitted reports a sufficient review of the safety was able to be conducted.
Data:

The safety of treatment with tafasitamab in combination with lenalidomide and as
monotherapy was evaluated on the basis of the following:

x

geography)

Version date: July 24, 2019 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)

Reference ID: 4650005

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation BLA 761163
Monjuvi (tafasitamab-cxix)
Adverse events and medical history terms were re-coded in the integrated ADaM datasets using
the MedDRA dictionary version 21.0. Laboratory and AE toxicities were graded according to
CTCAE, version 4.03.

Treatment-emergent adverse events (TEAEs) were defined as those adverse events that either
started or worsened in severity on or after the First Treatment Date/Time and on or before the
End of On-Treatment Phase Date/Time (i.e., during the On-Treatment Phase). Any AE that
occurred more than 30 days after the date/time of last dose of study treatment was considered
treatment emergent (TEAE). If the End of Treatment Date was missing, any AE occurring after
the treatment start date was considered treatment-emergent. As disease progression was
collected as an adverse event in trials MOR208C201 and MOR208C202, but not in trials
XmAb5574-01 and MOR208C03 (L-MIND), AEs of disease progression (identified as events with
preferred term = ‘Disease Progression’) were not included in the adverse event summaries.
The Applicant’s Position:
The approach to categorization of AEs was consistent with regulatory guidance and appropriate
for evaluation of the safety data.
The FDA’s Assessment:
A TEAE was defined as any AE reported from the first date of administration of study drug to
the last date of administration of study drug + 30 days. The FDA agrees with above.
Routine Clinical Tests
Adverse events could be directly observed, reported spontaneously by the patient or elicited by
questioning the patient at each study visit. Per protocol, patients were to be questioned in a
general way, without asking about the occurrence of any specific symptoms. In addition, AEs
could also be detected when reported by the subjects during or between visits or through
physical examination, laboratory test results, or other assessments. Adverse events of Special
Interest (AESIs) were selected based on the mechanism of action of tafasitamab and
lenalidomide as well as nonclinical and early clinical observations. Laboratory data from all
sources (central and local laboratories) were combined as described in the ISS Statistical
Analysis Plan.
The hematological parameters in relation to Grade 3 or more anemia, leukopenia, neutropenia,
and thrombocytopenia were summarized as the number and percent of patients demonstrating
any such value at least once during the On-Treatment Phase in the pivotal MOR208C203 (LMIND) trial, and for the tafasitamab monotherapy studies.
Evidence of drug-induced liver injury was sought by review of ALT, AST, bilirubin and alkaline
phosphatase data and by respective MedDRA PTs.

Monjuvi (tafasitamab-cxix)
The Applicant’s Position:
The clinical monitoring and summarization of patient safety (methods and time points)
described in the protocols and in the ISS Statistical Analysis Plan followed a standard approach
and were appropriate for a comprehensive assessment of the safety of tafasitamab in
combination with lenalidomide and as monotherapy for the target population and indication.
The FDA’s Assessment:
For the pivotal trial, MOR208C203, central laboratory values were collected at screening and on
day 1 of each treatment cycle. Local laboratory values were done at other times up to 24 hours
before administration of study drugs. These local laboratory values were not collected in the
clinical database. Investigators were asked to report clinically significant laboratory
abnormalities based on central and local laboratory test results as adverse events which
resulted in higher reporting rates of laboratory AEs in the AE dataset as compared to the lab
dataset.

(b) (6)

Patient
(MOR208C201) experienced 3 TEAE with fatal outcome, namely (i)
sudden and severe pneumonia accompanied by (ii) respiratory failure and (iii) cardiac
failure in Cycle 1 (Study Day 23). The patient died on the same day.
(b) (6)
Patient
(MOR208C202) developed sclerosing cholangitis on Study Day 97 and
died one month later on Study Day 124 due to fulminant liver failure. A causal
relationship of the event to tafasitamab could not be excluded; however, the patient
suffered from intercurrent acute hepatitis A, which was diagnosed 10 days prior to
death, and had suspected acute Graft versus Host Disease (GvHD) after bone marrow
transplant starting approximately 3 months prior to initiating study medication. The
GvHD involved the gastrointestinal tract and liver, either of which could lead to liver
failure. However, a causal relationship between death and study medication cannot be
ruled out.
(b) (6)
Patient
(MOR208C202) experienced sepsis with symptoms of hypotension,
respiratory distress and acute kidney injury, secondary to sepsis on Study Day 33. The
event was likely to have been caused by Stenotrophomonas maltophilia infection. The
patient died two days after event onset (Study Day 35).
(b) (6)
Patient
(MOR208C202) developed sepsis with hypotension on Study Day 20.
Multi-organ system failure progressed, and patient died 23 days after event onset
(Study Day 43). The event was considered rather to have been caused by progression of
disease.
(b) (6)
Patient
(MOR208C203, L-MIND, Combination Therapy phase) experienced
cerebrovascular accident with left-sided hemiplegia secondary to stroke on Study Day
109 and concurrently developed Grade 4 pulmonary embolism and Grade 4 deep vein
thrombosis two days prior to stroke. Although the investigator stated biological
evidence for disease progression, the stroke with fatal outcome (Study Day 111) was not
just a consequence of disease progression, but rather potentially due to multiple
reasons, including prolongation in bed, underlying comorbidities and the inability to
administer preventive treatment with low molecular weight heparin.
(b) (6)
Patient
(MOR208C203, L-MIND, Combination therapy phase) experienced
sudden death at home on Study Day 17 of Cycle 1. The event was likely to have resulted
from cardiovascular comorbidities, to which the patient was predisposed due to
treatment with R-CHOP twice, as frontline therapy for DLBCL as well as for the first
relapse. No additional relevant details were available.
(b) (6)
Patient
(MOR208C203, L-MIND, Combination therapy phase) experienced
multifactorial respiratory failure on Study Day 43 in the context of a respiratory
superinfection (adenovirus infection), neutropenic fever, pleural effusion of neoplastic
nature and mixed acidosis. The event was likely to have resulted from suspected
lymphoma progression (pleural fluid was of neoplastic nature). The patient died 8 days
after event onset (Study Day 51).

The Applicant’s Position:
A review of the deaths across the pivotal MOR208C203 trial and the 3 tafasitamab
monotherapy trials (MOR208C201, MOR208C202, and XmAb5574-01) indicated that most
ĚĞĂƚŚƐ ;ϯϴͬϱϬ͕ ϳϲйͿ ǁere due to disease progression. Of the remaining 8 fatalities caused by
TEAEs, 6 were related to infections, a pattern seen similarly with other B cell depleting
antibodies, although none of these were considered to be related to study medication by the
investigators. The only death for which a causal relationship to tafasitamab could not be
excluded was a case of sclerosing cholangitis confounded by acute hepatitis A and Graft versus
Host Disease after bone marrow transplant that started approximately 3 months prior to start
of study medication in study MOR208C202.

were noted for deaths within 60 days of first study treatment.

The clinical reviewer reviewed the 8 narratives regarding deaths within 30 days of last dose for
trial MOR208C203. Below is a summary of these narratives.
(b) (6)

Patient
was a 81-year-old woman with DLBCL with ECOG score of 2 at screening. The
patient had a PMH of TIA, hyperthyroidism, renal failure, PE, epilepsy, PRES, and hypokalemia.
At the time of enrollment she had hypertension, atrial fibrillation, vertigo, AV block,
hypogammaglobulinemia, anemia, thrombocytopenia, urinary tract infection and
hypomagnesemia, Adverse events and SAEs during the trial included diarrhea, (SAE),
neutropenia, muscular weakness (SAE), CMV infection (SAE), febrile neutropenia (SAE), and
urinary tract infection. The patient was hospitalized on day 93 due to febrile neutropenia which
was prolonged due to a grade 4 DVT and pulmonary embolism on day 107, the following day
the patient had acute renal failure. Anticoagulation was not possible due to thrombocytopenia.
Two days later the patient had a CVA. The last dose of tafasitamab was day 99 and the last dose
of lenalidomide was day 93. A CT on day 109 did not confirm evolution of lymphoma, however,
the investigator felt lymphoma lesions in the leg were evolving. The patient received palliative
care on day 109. The patient died on day 111. The probable cause of death was reported as
CVA, an autopsy was not done.

Patient
was a 75-year-old women with DLBCL, stage IV with prior treatment of 1 line

discontinued and the patient died on day 214 with a cause of death reported as diffuse large B
cell lymphoma.

The Applicant’s Position:
These results indicate risks which are consistent with the target population of patients with
R/R-DLBCL receiving B-cell depleting therapy.

treated. There were no cases of tumor lysis syndrome in MOR208C203 in target population.
The FDA’s Assessment:
Regarding the above analysis, for Table 36 a causal relationship to tafasitamab was suspected.
In a single arm trial, attribution of causality is difficult and all AEs regardless of attribution
should be considered for evaluation of safety. For trial MOR208C203 for an SAE to be included
in this analysis the TEAE had to be considered not related to lenalidomide. The serious adverse
events, limited to 1 per patient at the TEAE and at the System Organ Class level, obtained by

The Applicant’s Position:
A low number of patients discontinue tafasitamab due to a TEAE. The pattern of TEAEs leading

The FDA’s Assessment:
The clinical reviewer obtained similar data to that presented above. A total of 20 patients in
trial MOR208C203 discontinued study treatment, either both drugs or either drug alone. In
reviewing the TEAE that lead to withdrawal of study medication for either drug or both, on trial
MOR208C203 neutropenia was the most frequent TEAE reported for 3 patients;

The pattern of TEAEs leading to dose modification (reduction or interruption) is consistent with

Significant Adverse Events
Three specific risks were identified which are explained in in detail in Section 8.2.5.
Other significant adverse events leading to modification and/or discontinuation of study
medication are described in detail under Dropouts and/or Discontinuations Due to Adverse
Effects in this section.
The Applicant’s Position:
The most common and significant AEs were linked to myelosuppression and infections; infusion
related reactions were seen in R/R DLBCL patients treated with tafasitamab/lenalidomide. The
proposed USPI recommends pre-medication including antipyretics, histamine H1 receptor
blockers, and glucocorticosteroids.
Overall, the adverse events seen in the clinical development program of tafasitamab are
consistent with the mode of action of a B-cell depleting drug in the target population.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA agrees that myelosuppression, infections, and infusion related reactions are significant
adverse events and these will be Warnings and Precautions in the U.S. Prescribing Information.

Severity of Treatment Emergent Adverse Events
The Grade 3 and 4 TEAEs in the safety analysis are presented in Table 42. The most frequently
occurring Grade 3/4 TEAEs are hematological (neutropenia, thrombocytopenia, leukopenia,
anemia, febrile neutropenia).
When patients completed the tafasitamab/lenalidomide therapy phase and continued on
tafasitamab monotherapy the frequency of Grade 3/4 TEAEs decreased.

Adverse Drug Reactions
To define ADRs, the treatment-emergent adverse events were reviewed taking into
consideration temporal association, biological plausibility, evidence of positive de-challenge
and re-challenge where available, consistency, causality assessment by investigators and
alternative explanations for adverse events. Study MOR208C203 was used to determine the
frequency of ADRs.
For TEAEs reported during the combination therapy phase in MOR208C203 (L-MIND) both
lenalidomide and tafasitamab had to be considered as suspect drugs for a causality assessment.
Table 44 summarizes commonly reported adverse reactions in MOR208C203 (L-MIND) overall,
in the combination therapy and tafasitamab monotherapy phases separately, and in the
tafasitamab monotherapy trials individually and pooled.

Laboratory Findings
Hematology parameters to be summarized included platelets, hemoglobin, WBC count,
absolute neutrophil count, and lymphocytes; blood chemistry parameters included sodium,
magnesium, potassium, calcium, AST, ALT, serum creatinine, BILI, and ALP. Shift tables using
CTCAE grades to compare baseline to the worst post-baseline CTCAE grade were produced for
applicable hematology and biochemistry laboratory parameters. The grades for laboratory
parameters are based on the numeric component of the CTCAE grading scale v4.0. Per protocol,
abnormal laboratory values considered clinically significant by the investigator were to be
reported as AEs.
Changes in hematological parameters were analyzed for patients with both baseline and
post baseline values. Overall, the observed changes in MOR208C203 were consistent with the
reported hematologic TEAEs:

Vital Signs
Vital sign parameters included heart rate (HR), systolic (SBP) and diastolic blood pressure (DBP),

into standard international (SI) units for summary. The number and percentage of patients
meeting the abnormal vital sign criteria by visit and at any time during the On-Treatment Phase
were summarized.

The number and percent of patients with notable abnormalities (i.e., individual outlier values
based on prespecified criteria) were similar between the pooled monotherapy trials and
MOR208C203.

Electrocardiograms (ECGs) and QT
An analysis of recordings from patients enrolled in L-MIND did not reveal any abnormal QTc
prolongation or any other electrocardiographic abnormality potentially associated with the
study drug.
Immunogenicity
The clinical assessment of immunogenicity for tafasitamab was evaluated in 245 patients, of
whom ϴϭ ;ϯϯ͘ϭйͿ ŽĨ ƚŚĞ Ϯϰϱ ƉĂƚŝĞŶƚƐ ǁĞƌĞ ƉĂƌƚ ŽĨ DKZϮϬϴϮϬϯ͘ dŚĞ ŽƚŚĞƌ ϭϲϰ ;ϲϲ͘ϵйͿ
patients were derived from the four other clinical trials (XmAb5574-01, MOR208C201,
MOR208C202, and MOR208C205 (COSMOS), in which tafasitamab was tested in different
indications and/or different treatment settings compared with MOR208C203. In MOR208C203,
mean exposure time to tafasitamab was 49.3 weeks, and anti-drug antibody (ADA) samples
were collected for up to two years.
A low number of patients ;ϭϳ ΀ϲ͘ϵй΁ ŽĨ ƚŚĞ Ϯϰϱ ƉĂƚŝĞŶƚƐ overall) tested ADA-positive before
start of tafasitamab treatment, suggesting the presence of pre-existing antibodies. Only six of
these patients with pre-existing ADAs were ADA-positive on an intermittent basis after start of
tafasitamab treatment (i.e., ADA positive at one to seven sampling time points). The other 11 of
these patients with pre-existing ADAs did not show any positive ADA assessment during
treatment. No patients testing negative before the start of tafasitamab treatment subsequently
tested positive. Thus, in all five clinical trials, no treatment-emergent or treatment-boosted
ADAs could be detected. The ADA titers were low, and there was no apparent clinical impact of
ADAs on PK, safety, or efficacy.
The Applicant’s Position:
No clinically significant immunogenicity was detected for tafasitamab.
The FDA’s Assessment:
Refer to section 6.2.1 immunogenicity.

8.2.5. Analysis of Submission-Specific Safety Issues
Specific AEs related to infusion-related reactions, myelosuppression, and infections were
identified as part of the comprehensive safety review during the development of tafasitamab in
combination with lenalidomide.
Myelosuppression
Treatment with tafasitamab can cause serious or severe myelosuppression including
neutropenia, thrombocytopenia, and anemia. In patients treated with tafasitamab and

x
x

x

One 90-year-old male patient in MOR208C201 suffered genital herpes zoster which was

study drug. Confounding factor was reported as decreased immunity due to the
underlying leukemia. The patient recovered.

suspected to be associated with colitis. The investigator assessed as related to
tafasitamab, not related to lenalidomide; the patient recovered.

Infection events with a fatal outcome
In the pooled monotherapy trials, three patients had infection events with a fatal outcome, all
in Cycle 1; all three deaths were not considered related to tafasitamab (Table 35):
x
x
x

One patient died from Stenotrophomonas maltophilia sepsis.
One patient died from Enterococcus faecium sepsis in multi-organ failure.
One patient died from pneumonia in respiratory and cardiac failure.
(b) (6)

Patient
(MOR208C203) experienced multifactorial respiratory failure on study day 43
in the context of a respiratory superinfection (adenovirus), neutropenic fever, pleural effusion
of neoplastic nature and mixed acidosis. The event was likely to have resulted from suspected
lymphoma progression (pleural fluid was of neoplastic nature). The patient died 8 days after
event onset (Study Day 51).
In the MOR208C203 study, one patient experienced a worsening of progressive multifocal
leukoencephalopathy with a fatal outcome. The patient first developed unspecific neurological
symptoms ten days prior to initiation of study treatment, which worsened while on study drug.
The case was assessed as not related to any study drug. The patient died more than 30 days
after cessation of study therapy.
Infections are considered an identified risk of treatment with tafasitamab. Most patients
recovered after receiving standard antibiotic treatment. The five patients who died were
severely debilitated with underlying comorbidities that predisposed them to risk of infection
with poor outcome.

The Applicant’s Position:
Specific safety issues of tafasitamab concern adverse events which are consistent with the
targeted patient population and the pharmacological mode of action of a B-cell depleting drug.:
Adverse events linked to myelosuppression, in particular neutropenia, anemia and
thrombocytopenia are the primarily expected events with the combination therapy with
tafasitamab and lenalidomide due to its B-cell depleting properties.
Infections are expected events with the combination therapy with tafasitamab and
lenalidomide due to its B-cell depleting properties. The most common infections are respiratory
tract and urinary tract infections. High Grade infections and poor outcomes are also considered
to be due to the underlying conditions of the patients.

BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation BLA 761163
Monjuvi (tafasitamab-cxix)
Infusion related reactions events were observed, all of which resolved without serious
sequelae. Infusion related reactions typically occurred during the first infusion and tended to be
mild in severity. All patients in the clinical trials received standard prophylactic treatment which
appears to be sufficient to manage these reactions.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA agrees that infusion related reactions, myelosuppression, and infections are important
safety risks with tafasitamab. The FDA analysis of the data from the L-MIND trial will be
included in the USPI.

8.2.6. Clinical Outcome Assessment (COA) Analyses Informing
Safety/Tolerability
Patient-reported outcomes were not included in the trials of tafasitamab with or without
lenalidomide.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The clinical reviewer is in agreement.
8.2.7. Safety Analyses by Demographic Subgroups
Subgroup evaluations of TEAEs were conducted for patients in MOR208C203 to identify
potential safety issues in particular subpopulations defined by intrinsic/extrinsic factors: age,
sex, race, geography, and manufacturing process. Findings are as follows:
Age
Among 80 patients treated with tafasitamab in combination with lenalidomide in MOR208C203,

^ĞƌŝŽƵƐ ĞǀĞŶƚƐ ŽĐĐƵƌƌĞĚ ŝŶ Ϯϭͬϰϯ ;ϰϴ͘ϴйͿ ŵĂůĞ ƉĂƚŝĞŶƚƐ ĂŶĚ ŝŶ ϭϰͬϯϳ ;ϯϳ͘ϴйͿ ĨĞŵĂůĞ ƉĂƚŝĞŶƚƐ͘
The incidences of serious neutropenia, thrombocytopenia and infections were similar between
make and female patients.
Race
Of the 74 patients receiving combination therapy in MOR208C203 who self-reported race,
72 identified as White and two identified as Non-white. In the pooled monotherapy trials, a
total of 131 patients were White and ten patients were Non-White. As the number of
Non-White patients is very small, meaningful comparisons could not be made with respect to
adverse event patterns between these two groups.
Geography
In the MOR208C203, six patients were from the U.S., and 75 patients were from Europe.
Consequently, numbers of patients from the U.S. were too small to provide meaningful
comparisons of TEAEs by geographic region.

8.2.9. Additional Safety Explorations
Human Carcinogenicity or Tumor Development
In MOR208C203, the median time on tafasitamab in combination with lenalidomide was
190.0 days (range: 3 to 436 days). The cumulative dose administered ranged from 1,567.9 to
88,854.6 mg for tafasitamab and 75.0 to 6,300.0 mg for lenalidomide. The proportion of
ƉĂƚŝĞŶƚƐ ŝŶ DKZϮϬϴϮϬϯ ǁŚŽ ƌĞĐĞŝǀĞĚ шϭϮ ĐǇĐůĞƐ ŽĨ ƚĂĨĂƐŝƚĂŵĂď ŵŽŶŽƚŚĞƌĂƉǇ ǁĂƐ ϰϮ͘Ϭй͘ KŶĞ
patient in MOR208C203 completed 1,170 days (167.1 weeks) of tafasitamab treatment and one
patient in MOR208C201 completed 1,998 days (285.4 weeks) of tafasitamab treatment
(Table 31). The following five preferred terms were reported in the Combination Therapy phase
in MOR208C203, with no event reported in more than one patient: osteoma, prostatic
adenoma, squamous cell carcinoma, benign neoplasm of thyroid gland, and lipoma. In the
tafasitamab Monotherapy phase, the events of marginal zone lymphoma recurrent and basal
cell carcinoma were reported.

190
Version date: July 24, 2019 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the Applicant
and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.
Reference ID: 4650005

BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation BLA 761163
Monjuvi (tafasitamab-cxix)
The Applicant’s Position:
Treatment-emergent AEs of neoplasia were observed with tafasitamab with or without
lenalidomide. Carcinogenicity studies have not been conducted, and limited long-term data
preclude definitive evaluation of carcinogenicity.
The FDA’s Assessment:`
The safety database was reviewed for possible secondary malignancies. For trial MOR208C203
a 58-year-old man developed basal cell carcinoma, a 71-year-old man developed prostatic
specific antigen increase, a 81-year-old man developed squamous cell carcinoma, an 82-yearold man developed paraneoplastic syndrome and a 68-year-old woman was had a TEAE of
myelodysplastic syndrome. On trial MOR208C201 a 69-yearold man had a TEAE of
myelodysplastic syndrome and on trial XmAb5574-01 a 84-year-old man had a TEAE of pituitary
tumor. Regarding trial MOR208C203 and secondary malignancies, as this is a single arm trial
which included lenalidomide, attribution to tafasitamab cannot be determined.
Human Reproduction and Pregnancy
No pre-clinical reproductive and developmental toxicity studies have been conducted to date.
The teratogenic potential of tafasitamab with or without lenalidomide has not been
established. There are no data on use in pregnant women.
The Applicant’s Position:
Use in pregnant women is not recommended.
The FDA’s Assessment:
Tafasitamab is used in combination with lenalidomide and the use of lenalidomide is
contraindicated in pregnant women. This will be stated in the U.S. Prescribing Information.
Pediatrics and Assessment of Effects on Growth
Diffuse large B-cell lymphoma is a cancer of B cells, a type of lymphocyte that is responsible for
producing antibodies. It is the most common form of non-Hodgkin lymphoma among adults,
with an annual incidence of 7–8 cases per 100,000 people per year in the U.S. This cancer
occurs primarily in older individuals, with a median age of diagnosis of 65 years, although it can
occur in young adults and, in rare cases, children. Conducting pediatric studies for tafasitamab
with or without lenalidomide in the treatment of relapsed or refractory DLBCL is highly
impracticable.

The safety and effectiveness of tafasitamab in pediatric patients has not been established.

BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation BLA 761163
Monjuvi (tafasitamab-cxix)
Overdose, Drug Abuse Potential, Withdrawal, and Rebound
There is currently no information available regarding overdose with tafasitamab with or
without lenalidomide. The highest dose that has been administered is 12.0 mg/kg, and no doselimiting toxicity has been observed. Drug abuse and dependence with tafasitamab with or
without lenalidomide have not been studied and is not likely. No studies have been conducted
to specifically assess withdrawal or rebound effects of tafasitamab with or without
lenalidomide.
The Applicant’s Position:
In the event of overdose, patients should be observed, and appropriate supportive care should
be given, if required. Given the nature of tafasitamab and its IV administration by a licensed
health care professional, no abuse or dependence is anticipated. Given the disease being
treated and the nature of tafasitamab, no withdrawal or rebound effects are anticipated.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The clinical reviewer agrees with the statement above.

SUMMARY AND CONCLUSIONS
8.1.

Statistical Issues

The FDA’s Assessment:
The activity of tafasitamab in combination with lenalidomide (tafa+LEN) was investigated in
Study MOR208C203 (L-MIND), a single arm study. Per FDA’s analysis, the estimated ORR was
55й ;ϵϱй /͗ ϰ3й͕ ϲ7йͿ in patients with centrally-confirmed DLBCL. Because this is a
combination therapy, the contribution of effect question that is being raised is whether the
observed ORR 55й is attributable to lenalidomide (in part or in whole). Per the MOR208C201
study, an estimated investigator-assessed ORR for single agent tafasitamab was approximately
Ϯϲй ;ϵϱй /͗ ϭϯй, ϰϯйͿ in DLBCL patients. The Applicant also cited single agent lenalidomide
(LEN) data from the literature showing ORR estimates that ƌĂŶŐĞ ĨƌŽŵ Ϯϭй ƚŽ Ϯϵй͘ As the
Applicant noted, the comparison of trial results with external results to learn about
contribution of effect is not straightforward for a number of reasons, including differences in
the target population.
The primary objective of Study MOR208C206 (RE-MIND) was to learn about LEN single agent
activity in DLBCL patients in non-clinical trial settings. The RE-MIND study induced a LEN
external cohort that resembled patients in the tafa+LEN cohort from the L-MIND trial and
compared their estimated ORRs, with a view towards eliciting supportive information to clarify
the contribution of effect question. At face value, the results of the RE-MIND study suggested
that tafa+LEN is a substantial improvement over LEN as the respective ORRs per FDA’s analyses
were ϱϵй ;ϵϱй /͗ ϰϲй͕ ϳϭйͿ ĂŶĚ ϯϮй ;ϵϱй /͗ Ϯϭй͕ ϰϯйͿ͘ Had this been a randomized study,
these results would resolve the contribution of effect question. In an observational study
context however, the interpretation of these RE-MIND results are not straightforward as they
are likely to be contaminated by external factors, some of which include

This raises the question of whether the difference in the size of the estimated ORRs between
the tafa+LEN and LEN cohorts are credible or whether the difference can be ascribable to these
contaminants, in part or in whole. Unfortunately, these issues cannot be resolved in an
unambiguous way. FDA performed additional analyses to address some of these issues.
Outcome Misclassification
Outcome misclassification occurs when the reported response data are not correct. For
example, a patient may be incorrectly reported as a non-responder when in fact the patient
was a responder (false negative). Similarly, a patient may be incorrectly reported as a
responder when in fact the patient was a non-responder (false positive). The L-MIND trial
captured both IRC- and INV- assessed disease status, which enabled an assessment of ORR
based on IRC. In the RE-MIND study, the response in the LEN cohort was physician-reported.
As part of the RE-MIND study, some LEN patients were selected for a validation study where
their disease status were retrospectively assessed by a review committee. Although the
validation study sampled approximately 70 patients, the study was small in the sense that only
22 physician-reported responders were sampled; thus, the utility of the results of the validation
study is an open question. It is likely that the outcome misclassification process is differential
between the tafa+LEN and LEN cohorts in the RE-MIND study.
As outcome misclassification is likely to be differential between patients in the LEN and
tafa+LEN cohorts, FDA conducted additional analyses to assess the variability of the RE-MIND
results . The setting for the analyses assumes that IRC-assessed response status is the desired
endpoint while physician-reported response status is subject to potential misclassification. This
implies that the response status of patients in the LEN cohort who were not in the validation
study were considered subject to misclassification. Assuming a true positive of no less than

estimated adjusted ORR of 39й ;ϵϱй /͗ Ϯϲй͕ ϱϭйͿ ĨŽƌ ƚŚĞ LEN cohort. This resulted in an ORR

Residual Confounding
Residual confounding stems from the fact that regardless of attempts to analytically address
confounding when comparing two cohorts in an observational study, the efforts may not be
sufficient. Reasons for the insufficiency include the following.
Prognostic for ORR. The nine factors used in the RE-MIND study are potentially prognostic for
OS/PFS. However, there appears to be little data to suggest that they are prognostic for ORR. To
the extent that this is true, achieving balance on these nine covariates would not address the
issue of confounding. At this time, it is unclear that the nine covariates are prognostic for ORR.
Remaining Imbalance. Assuming that the factors being controlled for are prognostic for ORR, it
is clear that some of the attributes were not balanced. For example, the marginal distribution of
Ann Arbor staging, ECOG, and number of prior lines were not balanced and were more
favorable to the tafa+LEN cohort. To this point, FDA performed an additional propensity score
analysis (results not shown) on the MAS25 analysis set to induce greater balance on all 9
covariates. The results of this analysis were similar to the Applicant’s results and thus did not
change the general conclusion. However, the similarity of results between FDA and the
Applicant’s analyses should be interpreted carefully as it could simply mean that the covariates
were weakly or not prognostic for ORR.
We also note that similarity in the marginal distributions is not sufficient to justify claims of
achieving balance as it must also be supported by similarity in the joint distributions. Due to the
small size of the RE-MIND study, it is not feasible to adequately assess similarity of the joint
distributions of baseline characteristics. However, we noted in Section 8.1.3 that the joint
distributions of refractory to last line, number of prior lines, and primary refractory disease do
not appear to be balanced in the MAS25 matched analysis set.
Unmeasured Confounding. Even if it was feasible to perfectly balance the pre-specified factors,
the fact still remains that there are yet to be determined/unmeasured factors (e.g.,
cytogenetics) that are prognostic for ORR in the studied population and may be less favorable
to the LEN cohort. Addressing unmeasured confounding is analytically difficult despite methods
that suggest otherwise (e.g., Schneeweiss 2006; Lin et al 1998; VanderWeele et al 2017).
Furthermore, the results of such analyses are often over-interpreted. This difficulty stems from
the fact that the underlying assumptions cannot be rigorously justified and from the fact that it
is difficult to conclude whether the results of such analyses are plausible. Consider FDA Table 54
below. This table was obtain through an FDA simulation study that examined the sensitivity of
the observed results to various distributional scenarios of an unmeasured binary confounder,
denoted as U, across the tafa+LEN and LEN cohorts and across responders (ORR=1) and nonresponders (ORR=0). FDA’s analyses examined over 2700 scenarios. The results in FDA Table 54
were obtained after selecting only scenarios ǁŚĞƌĞ ƚŚĞ ϵϱй ĐŽŶĨŝĚĞŶĐĞ interval of the
difference in ORR rates contains 0, and after eliminating all scenarios that were viewed as not

8.1.

Conclusions and Recommendations

The FDA’s Assessment:
Study MOR208C203 (L-MIND) was the primary study upon which the activity of tafa+LEN was
assessed in DLBCL patients who had relapsed after or were refractory to at least 1, but no more
than 3 previous systemic regimens, and who were not candidates for high dose chemotherapy
and subsequent ASCT (or relapsed after ASCT). The results from the L-MIND study (per FDA’s
analyses) suggested that tafasitamab in combination with lenalidomide resulted in an
ĞƐƚŝŵĂƚĞĚ KZZ ŽĨ ϱϱй ;ϵϱй CI͗ ϰϯй͕ ϲϳйͿ͕ Z ŽĨ ϯϳй ;ϵϱй /͗ Ϯϱй͕ ϰϵйͿ͕ ĂŶĚ WZ Žf ϭϴй ;ϵϱй
/͗ ϭϬй͕ ϮϵйͿ in the 71 patients with central pathology confirmed diagnosis of
relapsed/refractory DLBCL patients. Estimated DOR was 21.7 months. Based on the ORR and
DOR and the lack of approved therapies for this patient population, the efficacy results from
the L-MIND trial are considered to provide a meaningful treatment benefit for the intended
patient population.
Additionally, the activity of single agent tafasitamab was assessed in Study MOR208C201, which
showed an estimated ORR of 26й ;ϵϱй /͗ ϭϯй, 43й). Although study MOR208C206 (REMIND), an observational study, was designed to learn about the contribution of effect question,
due to the limitations of the study however, it can only be concluded that the results from the
study regarding LEN activity were consistent with what was reported in the literature.
Based on the favorable risk-benefit profile of tafasitamab in combination with lenalidomide, the
clinical and statistical reviewers recommend approval of tafasitamab in combination with
lenalidomide for the treatment of adult patients with relapsed or refractory diffuse large B-cell
lymphoma (DLBCL) not otherwise specified, including DLBCL arising from low grade lymphoma,
and who are not eligible for autologous stem cell transplant (ASCT).

10. Pediatrics
The Applicant’s Position:
Tafasitamab received Orphan Drug Designation on December 1, 2014. Drugs with orphan drug
designation have a statutory exemption under Section 505B(k)(1) of the FD&C Act from the
requirement to conduct pediatric studies under the Pediatric Research Equity Act; therefore,
pediatric studies are not required for tafasitamab.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s assessment.

13. Postmarketing Requirements and Commitment
The FDA’s Assessment:
The final PMR and PMC language remains pending at this time. A PMR to confirm the benefit of
tafasitamab in a randomized controlled trial will be issued. A PMC will be issued to further
characterize the safety and efficacy of tafasitamab in combination with lenalidomide in the
underrepresented racial and ethnic minority patients with DLBCL.

19.2 Financial Disclosure
The Applicant’s Position:
The Applicant disclosed all financial interests/arrangements with clinical investigators as per 21
CFR Part 54. None of the investigators or sub-investigators involved in the clinical trials had
financial interests to disclose or arrangements that required disclosure. Limited instances of
missing financial disclosure information occurred for 6 sub-investigators across the studies. Of
these six, three did not see any patients while delegated on the study, one was delegated for
one month only, and information was missing for the remaining two.
The lack of disclosures across investigators in the clinical trials does not raise concerns about
the overall integrity of study data. The three studies were conducted over multiple sites and in
multiple countries, thus limiting the potential for a single investigator or sub-investigator to
make a significant contribution to the data. Further, all efficacy assessments were conducted by
an IRC, or both the Investigator and an IRC.

19.4 OCP Appendices (Technical documents supporting OCP
recommendations)
19.4.1
Summary of Bioanalytical Method Validation and
Performance
Tafasitamab concentrations in human serum were quantified using validated ligand binding
assays. The pivotal Study MOR208C203 (L-MIND) used the 2nd generation PK assay, an
electrochemiluminescence (ECL) assay based on MSD technology. Briefly, tafasitamab was
captured on the surface of a MSD plate coated with the Fab-fragment AbD18759.1 (human
monoclonal idiotypic anti-tafasitamab antibody), and the bound analyte was detected using the
antibody 6B11-E5-ECL, an ECL-labeled mouse monoclonal idiotypic anti-tafasitamab antibody.
The lower limit of quantification was 50 ng/mL in human serum. Other than Phase 1 Study
XmAb5574-01, this PK assay was used in all other clinical studies (L-MIND, COSMOS,
MOR208C201, MOR208C202). The PK assay has been validated for intended use. Summary
method performance of the bioanalytical method to measure tafasitamab in human serum,
with method performance in pivotal Study L-MIND, is shown in Table 55.

19.4.2 Pharmacometrics Review
A population pharmacokinetics (PPK) model was developed by the Applicant to characterize the
PK of tafasitamab in patients with either relapsed/refractory (R/R) chronic lymphocytic
leukemia (CLL)/small lymphocytic lymphoma (SLL), R/R Non-Hodgkin’s lymphoma (NHL)
(including diffuse large B-cell lymphoma (DLBCL)), or R/R acute lymphoblastic leukemia (ALL).
Patients intrinsic and extrinsic factors which influence the PK and PK variability of tafasitamab
were identified. Individual tafasitamab exposure metrics were generated for subsequent
exposure-response (E-R) analyses for efficacy and safety. In this review, the FDA
Pharmacometrics Reviewer verified the Applicant’s PPK model and E-R analyses for both safety
and safety.
1. Population PK analyses
Four clinical trials were included in the PPK analyses: XmAb5574-01, MOR208C201,
MOR208C202 and MOR208C203/L-MIND. In L-MIND, subjects received tafasitamab in
combination with lenalidomide (LEN), whereas in the other trials tafasitamab was administered
223
Version date: July 24, 2019 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the Applicant
and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.
Reference ID: 4650005

BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation BLA 761163
Monjuvi (tafasitamab-cxix)
as monotherapy. The final PPK analysis was conducted based on 221 subjects and 3972
measurable tafasitamab serum concentrations.
The intrinsic and extrinsic factors of sex, age, body weight, race, ethnicity, disease type, tumor
size, LDH, cancer stage, ECOG, B-cell count (time-varying), absolute lymphocyte count,
immunogenicity, total protein, ALB, liver enzymes, CrCL and LEN administration were analyzed
in the analysis.
The final model was a 2-compartment model with time-dependent elimination. The timedependent clearance was defined as CL*exp(-tɶ/(T50ɶ+tɶ))) to describe the increasing Cmin in LMIND trial observed beyond one year of treatment. The effect of baseline body weight (WTB)
was found to be significant on CL and V. The effects of ALB on CL, sex on V and disease type on
V were also included in the final PPK model. The final model parameters are shown in Table 56.
The covariate effects on exposures were generally moderate (5th percentile – 95th percentile of

Next, the Applicant derived exposure parameters (AUC0-28 (simulated cumulative exposure in

Reviewer’s comments: Overall, the Applicant’s PPK model appears reasonable. The significant
effect of body weight on PK, with higher CL and V expected at higher body weight, supports the
proposed mg/kg dosing regimen. The Applicant investigated the effect of renal function by
evaluating CrCL on CL as a continuous variable, and hepatic function by evaluating AST, ALT,
ALKP and GGT on CL as continuous variables. Upon information request, the Applicant
conducted additional sensitivity analysis to re-evaluate the effect of renal and hepatic
impairment using classification of renal and hepatic function (normal, mild, moderate, or
severe) as categorical variables.

were significant covariates. The sensitivity scm run resulted in the same covariates (i.e. body
