2. Exposure-response analyses for efficacy
The Applicant conducted exposure-response (E-R) analysis using ORR and PFS for each of the
studies separately (XmAb5574-01, MOR208C201 and MOR208C203/L-MIND). AUC0-28, AUC0-56
and maximal Cmin level were selected as exposure metrics for the current analysis, in which,
AUC0-28 is the most relevant PK measure. In study XmAb5574-01, the dose levels tested were
0.3 mg/kg, 1 mg/kg, 3mg/kg, 6 mg/kg, 9 mg/kg and 12 mg/kg, while in other two studies, the
tested dose level was 12 mg/kg only. In the following sessions, the E-R analyses for efficacy for
each study will be reviewed.
2.1 E-R analysis for efficacy of study XmAb5574-01
Twenty-seven (27) patients with R/R CLL/SLL were included in the analysis. The objective
response rate per dose level is shown in Figure 20. Overall, the ORR appears to increase in a
dose dependent manner, with highest response rate observed at 12 mg/kg QW dosing group.
Figure 20: Objective Response Rate Versus Dose Group in the Phase I Dose Escalation Study
XmAb5574-01

Source: Applicant’s exposure-response for efficacy study report MOR208I035

For the E-R analysis using PFS, a longer PFS for patients having higher exposures was observed
for all of the three PK metrics (Figure 21). In a sensitivity analysis that excluded the subjects

Reviewer’s comment: A positive E-R relationship for efficacy was observed in this FIH study.
However, very low number of subjects were enrolled in most of the dose cohorts in this study,
and the variability in the analysis for ORR and PFS is considered large. Thus, the observed E-R
relationship for ORR and PFS should be interpreted with caution.
2.2 E-R analysis for efficacy of study MOR208C201
Ninety-one (91) patients with different NHL subtypes were included in the analysis. All of the
subjects received 12 mg/kg QW tafasitamab for up to 3 cycles. Based on univariable logistic
regression, positive E-R relationships for probability of response were observed for all tested
exposure metrics. Figure 22 shows the univariable logistic regression result using AUC0-28 as the
predictor variable. A similar positive trend was observed in a sensitivity analysis, which
excluded 6 subjects who did not received the full number of planned doses during cycle 1 due
to early drop out (plots not shown in this review).

Reviewer’s comment: Overall, the Applicant’s E-R assessment for efficacy appears acceptable.

with the better efficacy (ORR and PFS). However, based on the distribution of available baseline
prognostic factors there appears to be some imbalance in IPI risk index, NK cell count and prior
lines for therapy across the exposure quartiles (Table 57 and Table 58). The patients in Q1
appears to be associated with higher IPI risk, lower NK cell counts, and more>3 prior lines of
therapy compared with those in Q4, which may have contributed to lower response in this group
and the overall positive E-R relationships for efficacy (Figure 22, Figure 23 and Figure 24). A
trend of higher in ORR was observed in patients with higher body weight (Figure 23), which may
be due to the higher exposure at higher body weight (Table 57).
2.3 E-R analysis for efficacy of study MOR208C203/L-MIND
In study MOR208C203/L-MIND, clinical effectiveness of tafasitamab was studied in combination
with LEN in subjects with R/R DLBCL. Tafasitamab was administered at a dose level of 12 mg/kg.
A slightly positive but not significant (p=0.093) E-R relationship was observed in a uni-variable
logistic regression using ORR versus AUC0-28 in the MOR208C203/L-MIND study. However, in a
sensitivity analysis that excluded 7 subjects who did not receive the full number of planned
doses during cycle 1 due to early drop out, a flat E-R relationship for OR from a uni-variable
logistic regression was observed (Figure 25). In addition, the Applicant noticed that ORR from
study MOR208C203/L-MIND were generally higher compared to that observed in study
MOR208C201. The covariate analysis did not reveal any pronounced dependency of ORR on
baseline characteristics (Figure 26).

dose of 12 mg/kg is reasonable in the combination therapy with LEN.
3. Exposure-response relationship for clinical safety
The Applicant conducted assessment for exposure-response for clinical safety. Adverse events
(AEs) were analyzed vs tafasitamab exposure for R/R NHL subjects (study MOR208C201,
including R/R DLBCL subjects) and R/R DLBCL subjects (study MOR208C203/L-MIND). AUC0-28
and Cmax were used in the analyses. The individual exposure metrics were separated into

Reviewer’s comment: The Applicant’s E-R analysis for safety appears acceptable. Apparent E-R
relationships were observed for several AESIs. However, the increase in odds ratio with
increasing exposure quartiles for these TEAEs was moderate, and the incidence rates per
exposure quartiles were low (<20%). Therefore, the Applicant’s proposed dosing regimen of
tafasitamab at a dose of 12 mg/kg in the combination with LEN is supported by the E-R
relationships for safety.
In conclusion, the Applicant developed a PPK model that reasonably described the PK behavior
of tafasitamab in patients with either R/R CLL/SLL, R/R NHL (including DLBCL), or R/R ALL. Body
weight had a statistically significant impact on both tafasitamab CL and V, supporting the body
weight-based dosing regimen. A positive E-R relationship was observed for tafasitamab
monotherapy clinical studies, while in combination with LEN, tafasitamab exhibited a flat E-R
relationship, whereas ORRs increased almost two folds compared to tafasitamab in
monotherapy. Based on above findings, the proposed dosing regimen for tafasitamab
administered at a dose of 12 mg/kg as IV infusion Q1W for three cycles and Q2W afterwards, in
combination with LEN is reasonable for patients with R/R-DLBCL.

19.4.3 Genomics
FCGR Polymorphisms and Tumor Response
FCGR2A and FCGR3A polymorphisms affecting the binding affinity of the Fc region of
therapeutic monoclonal antibodies to FCGRs have been reported to influence treatment
outcomes in different cancers (Mellor et al., 2013; Nagelkerke et al., 2019). According to the
Applicant, tafasitamab was engineered to allow increased binding affinity to FCGRs,
independent of the patient’s FCGR genotype. Binding affinities of tafasitamab for FCGR3A
(p.158V and p.158F alleles) were increased 47 and 130-fold relative to the unmodified IgG1
antibody and binding affinities for FCGR2A (p.131R and p.131H alleles) were increased 11 and
5.4-fold relative to the unmodified IgG1, respectively.
The Applicant conducted exploratory analyses of responses by FCGR genotype in studies
MOR208C203 (L-MIND) and MOR208C201 (DLBCL Subset). Germline DNA samples were
collected with mucosal cheek swabs from consented patients and centrally genotyped for
FCGR2A p.H131R (also denoted as p.H166R, rs1801274) and FCGR3A p.V158F (also denoted as
Ɖ͘sϭϳϲ&͕ ƌƐϯϵϲϵϵϭͿ ƉŽůǇŵŽƌƉŚŝƐŵƐ͘ ƉƉƌŽǆŝŵĂƚĞůǇ ϳϬй ŽĨ ƉĂƚŝĞŶƚƐ ŝŶ ^ƚƵĚǇ ϮϬϯ ĂŶĚ ϲϲй ŝŶ
Study 201 had evaluable samples with response assessment. Almost all patients were White in
both analyses.
